# Changelog

## 1.0.0-prerelease.5 — 2026-11-12

The fifth prerelease of envUI 1.0, and the last before it: the API pass before the 1.0 freeze, with each component's CSS of its own, then reviews of every component, preview and motion, and a refactor of the whole codebase. Code that only an option uses loads where the option is set, no style rule restyles the whole page as anything on it changes, and popups give focus back as they start to close, so a key pressed during the fade is not lost.

### Major Changes

- Two state attributes read as the rest do: the number of buttons at the end of Combobox's, DatePicker's, TimePicker's and MonthPicker's field is `data-action-count` (`1` or `2`), where it was `data-actions`, which Card sets as a flag; and TimeList's period column is `data-part="day-period"`, in kebab case, where it was `dayPeriod`.
- AvatarGroup's `data-gather` is `data-align`, `start` or `end`, after its `align` prop: select `[data-align='end']` where you selected `[data-gather='end']`.
- The layer behind a modal is the backdrop everywhere, as Base UI names it: `--env-color-bg-overlay` and `--env-color-bg-overlay-muted` are `--env-color-bg-backdrop` and `--env-color-bg-backdrop-muted`, `--env-z-overlay` is `--env-z-backdrop`, and Panes' `env-panes-scrim` is `env-panes-backdrop`, as Dialog's and Sheet's are. The theme boundaries are named after what they wrap: the notifications' `env-toast-portal-theme` is `env-toast-theme`, and `env-overlay-theme`, around the portal of a dialog, a sheet or the command palette, is the shared anatomy `Overlay` in the part contract.x class.
- NavigationBar names its parts as NavigationRail does: a destination's indicator is `env-navigation-bar-indicator` and its label `env-navigation-bar-label`, which were `env-bar-icon` and `env-bar-text`. Rename them in your style sheets and tests.
- Checkbox's color property for its check mark or dash, `--env-checkbox-icon`, is `--env-checkbox-selected-content`, the role every component gives what sits on its selected fill.
- The `@envsemble/envui/content` entry is two, named after what they hold: import Code and CodeBlock from `@envsemble/envui/code`, and Markdown, MarkdownEditor and their types from `@envsemble/envui/markdown`; an application that shows only code no longer installs react-markdown and remark-gfm ([setup](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/setup.md)).
- Every color token follows one grammar, `--env-color-{bg|fg|border}-{role}…`, where a component that names a color is its role:

  - the avatars' `--env-color-avatar-bg-*`, `--env-color-avatar-fg-*` and `--env-color-avatar-figure-*` are `--env-color-bg-avatar-*`, `--env-color-fg-avatar-*` and `--env-color-fg-avatar-figure-*`, the unsuffixed ones included;
  - `--env-color-list-item-bg`, `-hover` and `-pressed` are `--env-color-bg-list-item`, `-hover` and `-pressed`;
  - `--env-color-rating-star` is `--env-color-fg-rating-star` and `--env-color-skeleton-shine` `--env-color-bg-skeleton-shine`;
  - `--env-color-fg-link-on-tint` and its states are `--env-color-fg-link-on-muted`, for a link on a `*-muted` tint;
  - `--env-counter-badge-height` is `--env-badge-count-height`, for Badge's count.

  `--env-color-bg-navigation` is new, the transparent rest of a navigation item, beside its hover and pressed tokens.

- `ComboboxItem` reserves `items`: its type is `never`, since an entry of a Combobox's `items` with an `items` array is a group. An option type of the application's own that extends `ComboboxItem` with an `items` field no longer compiles; such an option was shown as a group. Rename the field, such as to `children` or `members`, and map the data before passing it to Combobox.
- Combobox names its start slots as other components do: the icon at the start of the field or an option, `env-combobox-leading`, is `env-combobox-start`, and the chosen option's icon in the field, `env-combobox-value-leading`, is `env-combobox-value-icon`.
- A multiple Combobox's `onValueChange` receives `(Item | ComboboxItem)[]` where it received `Item[]`: a chosen value none of the options has had, such as one given before a server search finds it, comes as a `ComboboxItem` of its own, with the value as its label, and never claimed the fields of your own `Item`. A handler typed for `Item[]` takes `(Item | ComboboxItem)[]` and tells the two apart, such as with `'email' in item`; with the default `ComboboxItem` nothing changes.
- A drag takes one state name for each meaning:

  - `data-dragging`, a continuous control's thumb on the move: Slider's field takes it while pressed, where it took `data-pressed`.
  - `data-moving="pointer"`, an item being reordered: DocumentTabs' dragged tab, where it took `data-dragging`, as List's rows do.
  - `data-resizing`, a handle that resizes: `env-resize-handle`, Sheet's and Drawer's, and Textarea's `env-textarea-resize-handle`, where they took `data-dragging`.
  - `data-drag-over`, a drop target under dragged files: DropZone, where it took `data-dragging`.

- "heading" names one part: the column of a title and its description, as in Dialog, PageHeader and PanelDock. The other `-heading` parts are renamed for what they are:

  - a title: Card's `env-card-heading` is `env-card-title`, Calendar's `env-calendar-heading` `env-calendar-title`, MonthPicker's `env-month-picker-heading` `env-month-picker-title` and DatePicker's `env-date-picker-time-heading` `env-date-picker-time-title`;
  - the row of a label and a value, or of a label and a button: Meter's `env-meter-heading`, Progress's `env-progress-heading`, Slider's `env-slider-heading` and CodeBlock's `env-code-block-heading` are `env-meter-header`, `env-progress-header`, `env-slider-header` and `env-code-block-header`;
  - a group's label, as in Menu and Combobox: NavigationDrawer's `env-drawer-heading` is `env-navigation-drawer-group-label` and CommandPalette's `env-command-palette-heading` is `env-command-palette-group-label`.

- Every icon's export takes the `Icon` prefix, so no icon shares its name with a component, such as `Link` or `Menu`: `import { IconAdd } from '@envsemble/envui/icons/Add'`. An icon's path stays its name; the numeric icons, whose exports had the prefix already, lose it from their path: `Icon10k` comes from `@envsemble/envui/icons/10k`. `Typography` is `IconTypography`, and `MaterialSymbolProps`, the props every icon takes, is `SymbolIconProps`. See [icons](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/icons.md).
- What Menubar.Menu's `onOpenChange` receives is `Menubar.MenuFocusDetails`, the element that had focus before the menubar took it, under a name no other menu's details share. `Menubar.MenuOpenChangeDetails` is gone: write `Menubar.MenuFocusDetails` in its place, the same details.
- NavigationBar, NavigationRail and NavigationDrawer name their parts and color properties after themselves. Rename them in your style sheets and tests:

  - NavigationBar: `env-bar`, `env-bar-list`, `env-bar-item`, `env-bar-indicator`, `env-bar-label`, `env-bar-count` and `env-bar-more` are `env-navigation-bar`, `env-navigation-bar-list`, `-item`, `-indicator`, `-label`, `-count` and `-more`; `--env-bar-item-container`, `--env-bar-item-content`, `--env-bar-item-icon`, `--env-bar-item-selected` and `--env-bar-item-selected-content` are `--env-navigation-bar-item-container`, `-content`, `-icon`, `-selected` and `-selected-content`.
  - NavigationRail: `env-rail`, `env-rail-header`, `env-rail-list`, `env-rail-item`, `env-rail-indicator`, `env-rail-label`, `env-rail-count` and `env-rail-footer` are `env-navigation-rail`, `env-navigation-rail-header`, `-list`, `-item`, `-indicator`, `-label`, `-count` and `-footer`; `--env-rail-item-container`, `--env-rail-item-content`, `--env-rail-item-icon`, `--env-rail-item-selected` and `--env-rail-item-selected-content` are `--env-navigation-rail-item-container`, `-content`, `-icon`, `-selected` and `-selected-content`.
  - NavigationDrawer: `env-drawer`, `env-drawer-header`, `env-drawer-list`, `env-drawer-section`, `env-drawer-group-label`, `env-drawer-item`, `env-drawer-label`, `env-drawer-count`, `env-drawer-branch`, `env-drawer-branch-row`, `env-drawer-toggle`, `env-drawer-chevron`, `env-drawer-children`, `env-drawer-footer` and `env-drawer-resize-handle` are `env-navigation-drawer`, `env-navigation-drawer-header`, `-list`, `-section`, `-group-label`, `-item`, `-label`, `-count`, `-branch`, `-branch-row`, `-toggle`, `-chevron`, `-children`, `-footer` and `-resize-handle`; `--env-drawer-item-container`, `--env-drawer-item-content`, `--env-drawer-item-icon`, `--env-drawer-item-selected` and `--env-drawer-item-selected-content` are `--env-navigation-drawer-item-container`, `-content`, `-icon`, `-selected` and `-selected-content`.

  Their state attributes, `data-background` (`page`), `data-current`, `data-resizable`, `data-env-skeleton` and Base UI's `data-starting-style` and `data-ending-style`, are unchanged on the renamed parts.

- Every component's root is `env-<component>`, and every part sits under its owner:

  - Dialog, ConfirmDialog and Sheet: the popup is `env-dialog` or `env-sheet`, which were `env-dialog-popup` and `env-sheet-popup`.
  - Textarea's root takes `env-textarea` too, beside `env-field` and `env-text-field`.
  - DocumentTabs: the root, `env-document-tabs`, is the bar that holds the strip and the end content, and takes `className`, `style` and `ref`, which reached the strip before. The strip, the tab list, is `env-document-tabs-list`; `env-document-tabs-root` and `env-document-tabs-bar` are gone.
  - MarkdownEditor's source text area, `env-markdown-source`, is `env-markdown-editor-input`, which

- A SegmentedControl's thumb says it shows with `data-placed`, once it has measured the chosen segment, rather than by losing its `hidden` attribute: it is hidden by its style until then. Restyle a shown thumb with `.env-segmented-control-thumb[data-placed]` in place of `.env-segmented-control-thumb:not([hidden])`.
- The color roles are named for one meaning each: the blues are primary, and the purple is accent.

  - Tokens: the blue `--env-color-bg-brand-*` and `--env-color-bg-accent-*` are `--env-color-bg-primary-*`, `--env-color-fg-accent` is `--env-color-fg-primary` and `--env-color-border-accent-*` is `--env-color-border-primary-*`. The solid accent badge fill is `--env-color-bg-primary-solid` and its soft tint `--env-color-bg-primary-soft`; `--env-color-bg-brand-muted` is `--env-color-bg-primary-muted`, the info alerts' tint. The purple `--env-color-bg-important-strong` is `--env-color-bg-accent-strong`, and `--env-color-fg-accent`, `--env-color-bg-accent-soft`, `-muted`, `-subtle` and `-solid` and `--env-color-border-accent` are its new purple tones.
  - Badge takes `tone="primary"`, the blue that `tone="accent"` was; `tone="accent"` is purple now, as Button's accent tone is. CalendarHeatmap's default tone is `primary`, the same blue, and it takes `accent` too.

- Every resize handle is a `-resize-handle` part, as Sheet's and Drawer's are: PanelDock's `env-panel-dock-resize` is `env-panel-dock-resize-handle`, Table's `env-table-column-resize` is `env-table-column-resize-handle`, and Textarea's `env-textarea-resize-edge` is `env-textarea-resize-handle`.
- The `floating` field variant is retired before 1.0. TextField and SearchField take `variant="outline"`, the default, or `"ghost"`, and `TextFieldVariant` is `'outline' | 'ghost'`; Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker no longer take `variant`, as their one look is the outline. The `--env-field-floating-height` token is gone with it, as is TextField's `[data-variant='floating']`, and so is `[data-tall]` on Combobox, DatePicker and Select, which only a floating field took; Textarea, MarkdownEditor and RichTextEditor keep it. The outline field's label still rests inside and floats into a notch in the outline.

  **Migration:** replace `variant="floating"` with the default outline and `size="lg"`, such as `<TextField size="lg" label="Owner" />`, and drop `variant` from Select, Combobox and the pickers, `variant="outline"` included ([TextField](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/text-field.md)).

- Scaffold names the navigation form of each window class in `data-navigation-compact`, `data-navigation-medium`, `data-navigation-expanded` and `data-navigation-large`, beside `data-navigation-form`, rather than `data-compact`, `data-medium`, `data-expanded` and `data-large`, which read as size states and clashed with Tree's `data-expanded`. Each takes `bar`, `rail`, `drawer` or `none`.
- SegmentedControl is segments in a recessed track, the chosen one raised on a thumb with a soft shadow, rather than Material's connected outlined segments: no outline, no lines between the segments and no check, and a chosen segment shows its icon. Tabs keep their indicator under the selected tab, so the two still read differently; the API stays. The check, `env-segmented-control-item-indicator`, is gone: select the chosen segment with `.env-segmented-control-item[data-checked]`. `--env-segmented-control-container` sets the track, the neutral fill by default, `--env-segmented-control-outline` an outline around the track, none by default, and `--env-segmented-control-selected` the thumb. At high contrast the thumb takes an edge in place of its shadow, and under forced colors the track an outline.
- Select's trigger is no Button any more, so it carries neither `data-variant` (`[data-variant='outline']`, `[data-variant='secondary']`) nor `data-size` (`[data-size='sm']`): select the field by `.env-select`, its size by `.env-select[data-size]` and its look by `.env-select-control[data-variant]`, as on a TextField.
- A chosen item's check is an `-indicator` part everywhere, as in Select, Combobox and the list rows: Menu's `env-menu-item-check-mark` is `env-menu-item-indicator`, in its place, `env-menu-item-check`, now `env-menu-item-indicator-slot`, and SegmentedControl's `env-segmented-control-item-check` is `env-segmented-control-item-indicator`.
- Spinner says its label is hidden as the fields do: it carries `data-label-hidden` while its label is hidden, the default, and nothing with `showLabel`, rather than `data-show-label` with it.
- The stacking levels say what they hold: `--env-z-popover`, for menus, popovers, hover cards and the lists of Select and Combobox, is `--env-z-popup`, and `--env-z-peek`, for a supporting pane over its main pane, is `--env-z-supporting-pane`.
- Every state attribute that is on or off is present, as `""`, or absent, as Base UI renders its own, so CSS selects it as `[data-x]`. These took `"true"`: Button's, ButtonGroup's, IconButton's and SegmentedControl's `data-full-width`; ColorPicker's `data-alpha`, `data-disabled`, `data-dragging`, `data-eye-dropper`, `data-invalid`, `data-readonly`, `data-selected` and `data-translucent`, and ColorSwatch's `data-translucent`; Composer's `data-running`, `data-disabled` and `data-start`; CodeBlock's `data-wrap`; Message's `data-text`; Progress's `data-segmented`; Toast's `data-limited`; TextField's `data-tall` and `data-label-hidden`; DatePicker's `data-label-floating`; CommandPalette's `data-empty`; PanelDock's `data-active`, `data-attention`, `data-filled`, `data-overflowing`, `data-resizing`, `data-scrolled`, `data-stacked` and `data-tabs`; RichTextEditor's `data-invalid`, `data-disabled` and `data-readonly`; TimeList's `data-centered`, `data-loop` and `data-disabled`. DocumentTabs' `data-preview` is present on a preview tab alone, where it took `"true"` or `"false"`.
- A ThemePreferenceProvider that applies to the document, outside any Theme, renders its boundary around the children as a plain element that takes the root's look and sets envUI's type again, as a boundary does, with none of a Theme's `env-theme` class and `data-env-theme`, `data-env-contrast` and `data-env-scale` attributes, which the root already carries: the same element before and after a pre-rendered page hydrates, so hydrating no longer restyles the whole page. Its theme, contrast and scale still reach nested Themes, popups and `useTheme()`. Style the application by the root's attributes, such as `[data-env-theme='dark'] .app`, rather than the boundary's. A provider given a `system` contrast while the page set a contrast of its own on the root keeps a boundary of its own.
- The TimeList row at the center of its wheel carries `data-centered`, in American spelling as every other name, rather than `data-centred="true"`.
- Every token has a description in the token list, and `--env-motion-ease-standard`, the same curve as `--env-motion-ease`, is gone: use `--env-motion-ease`, which
- Tree marks the row the keyboard is on with `data-highlighted`, as menus and lists do, rather than `data-active`, which names the tab or panel that shows.
- The typography tokens take the names of Text's variants, with `sm` as every other scale has it, and the category first:

  - type roles: `--env-type-h1` is `--env-type-headline`, `--env-type-h2` `--env-type-title` and `--env-type-body-small` `--env-type-body-sm`; `--env-type-display` is new, the display variant's font, which grows with the window;
  - font sizes: `--env-font-size-heading` is `--env-font-size-headline`, `-heading-small` `--env-font-size-title`, `-body-small` `--env-font-size-body-sm` and `-label-small` `--env-font-size-label-sm`;
  - `--env-body-font-weight` is `--env-font-weight-body`.

- The prop that hides a field's label is `visibleLabel`, named after the label it turns off, where it was `showLabel`: TextField, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker, MonthPicker and ColorPicker take `visibleLabel={false}` where they took `showLabel={false}`, and it still defaults to true. Spinner's and SearchField's `showLabel`, which shows a label that is hidden by default, stay as they are.

### Minor Changes

- Alert's `action` is `actions`, as on Card, Dialog, Sheet and Message, and List.Item's `action` is `end`, which pairs with its `start` as on CommandPalette's items.
- AnimatedNumber keeps the value it changed from while it leaves, `env-animated-number-previous`, which rolls out in `--env-motion-duration-fast` with `--env-motion-ease-exit` as the new value rolls in in `--env-motion-duration-medium` with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, so the number is never gone for a moment; with reduced motion they cross-fade. The leaving value is hidden from assistive technology and drawn from its `data-value`, so the text is still only the current value.
- AvatarGroup's `gather` is `align`, `'start'` or `'end'`, as Row, Popover and Table.Cell name the inline axis.
- Avatar, AvatarButton and AvatarGroup show a person's initials without a picture by default, so people tell their avatars apart, in place of the same default person picture for everyone; an empty name still shows the person picture. To keep the person picture, pass `fallback="person"`, such as for a guest; `fallback="initials"` is now the default and can go. In an AvatarGroup each avatar takes a ring in the color of the surface below, about 2 px, in place of its faint shadow, so the initials of avatars lying on one another never run together and the last avatar stays apart from the count. A Table rings them in its surface, with a hovered or selected row's fill over it.
- `SurfaceAppearanceProps` and `ColorPickerValueProps`, the building blocks of `SurfaceProps`, `ColorPickerProps` and `ColorPickerButtonProps`, are internal: type against the components' own props.
- CalendarHeatmap's `formatTooltip` is `renderTooltip`: it returns content, as Markdown's `renderLink` does, while every `format*` callback returns text.
- CalendarHeatmap's weeks start on the locale's first day, as Calendar's and DatePicker's do, and `weekStart` takes any `Weekday`, 0 to 6. They started on Sunday: pass `weekStart={0}` to keep that. CalendarHeatmap reads dates with Calendar's parser, so a date that doesn't exist, such as 2026-02-31, is left out and reported while developing, where it moved to the next month.
- Callbacks no longer take the name of a DOM event they don't behave like. CopyButton's `onCopy(text)` is `onCopied(text)`, and Composer's `onSubmit(value)` is `onSend(value)`, which pairs with `sendLabel`; A destination of Drawer, Rail and Bar runs `onClick(event)` instead of `onSelect()`, as BreadcrumbsItem does, and Menu.Item's `onClick` receives the event too.
- Choice controls take a field's words: RadioGroup a `description` and an `error`, which describe the group, Checkbox and Switch a `description` under the label, `env-checkbox-description` and `env-switch-description`, and Slider an `error`. A controlled RadioGroup's `value` takes `null` for none. SegmentedControl can be uncontrolled: `value` and `onValueChange` are optional, and `defaultValue` starts it, the first item by default.
- ColorPickerButton takes `open` and `onOpenChange`, as Popover does, so an application can close the picker once a swatch is chosen, and passes `ref`, ARIA, data and the other native props on to its button.
- ColorPicker's and ColorPickerButton's `copy` is `copyable`, as on CodeBlock; it still shows the copy button by default.
- ColorPicker's and ColorPickerButton's `onValueChange` receives the color's string in `valueFormat` alone, without React Aria's `Color` as a second argument, so a major release of React Aria's color packages needs none of envUI's. `value` and `defaultValue` still take a `Color`, and the picker keeps the hue of a gray itself.
- The `ThemeValue` type, `'light' | 'dark'`, is `ColorScheme`, as CSS calls it.
- `CommandPaletteActionOptions`, the second argument of CommandPalette's `onAction`, is `CommandPaletteActionDetails`, as every callback's extra argument is named.
- CommandPalette takes `loading` while its results are on their way, such as from a server. With none to show yet, its empty state says `loadingLabel` beside a spinner (Searching… by default, or `messages.commandPalette.loading`). Results already shown stay, marked busy, and the search field's icon turns into a spinner.
- CommandPalette's `defaultItems`, the options shown while the search field is empty, is `suggestedItems`: elsewhere, as in React, `default` names an uncontrolled starting value.
- Under a coarse pointer, CommandPalette hides the keys, its items' shortcuts and the key hints of its footer, which then shows only the application's own content, and shows a close button after the search field, `env-command-palette-close`, for touch screens that have no Esc, named by `messages.dialog.close`. The search and the button share a row, `env-command-palette-header`.
- The app scale defaults to `system`, a third value of `ThemeScale`: compact where the primary pointer is fine, such as a mouse or trackpad, and comfortable on touch. The stylesheet decides with `@media (pointer: fine)`, so a server render and the first paint already have the scale and nothing changes as the page hydrates. A `Theme` or `ThemePreferenceProvider` outside any Theme takes `system`, and `useTheme().scale` returns it; Theme writes `data-env-scale="system"`, and a root without a `data-env-scale` is `system` too. An explicit `scale="comfortable"` or `scale="compact"` wins on every pointer, and nested Themes and popups keep their boundary's scale as before. `createThemeBootstrapScript` takes `scale: 'comfortable'` as well as `'compact'`, and writes either to the root before the first paint; `system`, its default, writes nothing.

  **Migration:** an app that wants the comfortable sizes with a mouse too sets `scale="comfortable"` on its root `Theme` or `ThemePreferenceProvider`, and passes `scale: 'comfortable'` to `createThemeBootstrapScript` if it renders on the server ([Spacing & density](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/spacing.md)).

- Composer's `footer` is `description`, as on the fields: it is the text that describes the field, below the composer, where the `env-composer-footer` part still shows it.
- ConfirmDialog takes `confirmDisabled`, which disables its confirm button and keeps Enter in a field from confirming, such as for a confirmation that waits on a typed name.
- ConfirmDialog's `finalFocus` takes a `DialogFocusTarget`, as Dialog's and Sheet's do: a ref, or a function of how the dialog was opened, such as one that returns the row whose menu opened it.
- The contract data is importable: `@envsemble/envui/parts.json` (part classes, state attributes and custom properties), `@envsemble/envui/props.json` (every export's props) and `@envsemble/envui/tokens.json` (the token list, which the package now ships). Dev mode takes `import parts from '@envsemble/envui/parts.json'` where it can't fetch its own copy.
- Every primary button takes its tone's emphasis fill, as the default primary does: an accent primary keeps its saturated fill with a white label in light and takes a soft fill with a dark label in dark, so it is never louder than Continue beside it. The new tokens are `--env-color-bg-danger-emphasis-hover` and `-pressed`, and `--env-color-bg-accent-emphasis` with its `-hover` and `-pressed`. A count or diff stat in a danger primary button is an outlined pill in its label color, as in the other primaries.

  In dark, a warning alert rests on its softer tint, where its amber title and icon sank into the stronger one, and a secondary button among its actions is a lift of the text color rather than a tan tint.

- A destination takes an optional `id`, which names its Drawer section in `expanded` and `defaultExpanded` in place of its `href` or label, so the open sections keep their names when the labels are translated.
- The dev entry's `ProblemRule` is `LayoutProblemRule`, beside `LayoutProblem`, `LayoutOptions.minTextChars` is `minTextCharacters`, and `PartsEntry`, one entry of a `PartsFile`, is exported.
- While a Dialog or a Sheet is `pending`, every DialogClose inside it is disabled, as the built-in close button is, and its last action, the primary one, shows the pending state where it is a Button that sets no `pending` of its own, so `pending` no longer needs setting on each button.
- Dialogs follow the popups' rules: Dialog, ConfirmDialog and the dialogs built on them, such as MarkdownEditor's and RichTextEditor's Insert link dialog, take the popup's color, `--env-color-bg-popup` in light and `--env-color-bg-default` in dark, with the popups' soft shadow, `--env-popup-shadow`, and no outline edge. Unlike popups they stay solid, not frosted: over the scrim, what shows through only greyed the dialog and smudged it with the shapes behind. At high contrast, with `prefers-reduced-transparency` and under forced colors, a dialog has no shadow and its elevation edge returns, as a popup's does. No token changes.
- `--env-color-fg-disabled`, the label and value of a disabled control: quieter than muted text yet readable, in every theme. A disabled Button and TextField no longer fade as a whole, which left their text hard to read in dark: the label, value and icons take the disabled color, and the shape quiets more than the text, a filled button to half the secondary's neutral fill, an outline button and a field's outline to the muted border. Under forced colors they keep `GrayText`.
- DocumentTabs' items are `DocumentTabsItem`s, keyed by `value`, as `value` and `onValueChange` name the active tab, where `DocumentTab` had `id`; their native tooltip is `tooltip`, where it was `title`. DocumentTabs passes its native props, such as `id` and `data-*`, on to the strip. Breadcrumbs' item type is `BreadcrumbsItem`.
- DropZone's `onDropAccepted` and `onDropRejected` are `onFilesAccepted` and `onFilesRejected`: they run for browsed files as well as dropped ones.
- Fields are rounded rectangles rather than pills: TextField, SearchField, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker, MonthPicker and Field.Control take a text area's large corner, `--env-radius-lg`, no more than a third of the field's height, so a small field still reads as a rectangle. Buttons keep their round ends. The notched label is unchanged: the outline runs level into the notch past the corner. A ghost field takes the same corners, so a row of outline and ghost fields shares one corner; the command palette's search stays a pill. An editable ghost field shows a faint fill at rest, half the neutral fill, so a labelled one no longer reads as a label and its value; a read-only or disabled one keeps a fainter fill, quieter than one to fill in, and doesn't answer the pointer. With no edge to cut a notch in, a ghost field's label floats inside its fill, above the value, rather than across its top edge: a labelled single-line ghost field keeps the label, at the size of an outline field's notched label, within its height, so it is as tall as an outline field of its size, and a ghost Textarea starts its text below the label. A focused ghost field keeps its placeholder, as an outline field does. Outline fields keep their notched label.
- Popups are frosted: menus and submenus, Select and Combobox lists, the command palette, popovers, hover cards, the DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker popups, ColorPickerButton's popover and rich text suggestions show the popup's color at 88 % in light and 90 % in dark over a blur of what is behind them, with a soft shadow and no outline edge. Three properties, set on a theme, change them: `--env-popup-opacity`, `--env-popup-blur` and `--env-popup-shadow`, envUI's second drop shadow after the avatars'. At high contrast, with `prefers-reduced-transparency` and under forced colors, popups are solid, with their elevation edge and no blur or shadow. Tooltips stay solid.

  Inside them, rows are 40 px tall, 32 px at the compact scale, in body text with corners concentric with the popup's (1.25 rem on a menu or list); groups and Menu.Separator part by space, with no hairline, under a small muted label; shortcuts are muted text; a highlighted row takes the neutral fill and a chosen option the selection's fill as well as its check mark. The command palette's search is an opaque 3 rem pill with no outline, whose edge returns at high contrast.

  A Sheet has no edge line: its fill and its scrim set it apart, and the edge returns at high contrast.

- Tooltips are frosted, as the other popups are: their strong fill, `--env-color-bg-neutral-strong`, at 82 % over the popups' blur, with 0.75 rem corners, a roomier inset and a small soft shadow, `--env-tooltip-shadow`, in place of their edge. `--env-tooltip-opacity` and `--env-tooltip-shadow`, set on a theme, change them, as the popups' own properties do. At high contrast, with `prefers-reduced-transparency` and under forced colors a tooltip is solid, with its edge and no blur or shadow. A Kbd in a tooltip is a flat chip of the tooltip's text color with no edge, the keys of a combination side by side with the + read but not shown, and a tooltip opens 8 px from its control, as every popup does.
- envUI's text font is Geist, in place of Inter: `styles.css` loads one local variable face, `envUI Geist`, from regular (400) to bold (700), covering Latin, Latin-1 and Latin Extended-A, and `--env-font-family` names it. The file, `@envsemble/envui/assets/Geist.woff2`, replaces `assets/Inter.woff2` and is about half its size; `licenses/Geist-LICENSE.txt`, the SIL Open Font License, replaces Inter's. An application that refers to `envUI Inter` or the Inter file names `envUI Geist` and `Geist.woff2` instead; code keeps its monospace font.
- New: `Graphic`, an application's own logo, icon or picture at envUI's sizes, from a file in `src`, such as an imported PNG or SVG, or an SVG component as its children. `size` is `icon`, the default, the icon size of the slot it sits in, such as Button's `startIcon`, `text`, the size of the text around it, `sm`, `md`, `lg` and `xl`, Avatar's sizes, or `fill`, a block as wide as its container and as tall where the container has a definite height. The picture is contained and centered in that box, never stretched and never at the file's size; `fit="cover"` covers it instead, cropped, for a photo or a screenshot, and `aspect`, a ratio such as `"16/9"`, gives the box that shape, a step as its height and a fill from its width. It keeps its own colors, and `tint="text"` paints a one-color picture in the text color: an SVG component's shapes in `currentColor`, a file through a CSS mask, which a file from another origin needs CORS for. `alt` is required: it names the graphic as an image, and `alt=""` hides it as decoration. The parts are `env-graphic`, with `data-size`, `data-aspect`, `data-fit` and `data-tint`, `env-graphic-image` and `env-graphic-mask`. See [Graphic](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/graphic.md).

  `npx envui-graphic <file> [--out <dir>]` trims a file's empty edges once, when it joins the application: it crops a PNG, WebP or JPEG to its visible content, keeping its metadata, orientation and color profile, and fits an SVG's `viewBox` to its drawing. It writes the trimmed file beside the original as `<name>-trimmed`, or into `--out`, never over the original, and prints the Graphic to use; a JPEG or WebP is encoded again, at quality 95, and it says so. It needs `sharp`, which envUI doesn't install: add it as a dev dependency of the application, which it loads from, to run it.

  Every icon slot sizes and centers whatever it holds, an `img` or a component as well as an `svg`, to its icon size, so an application's own picture there no longer shows at its file's size. List.Item's and Combobox's `start` give an application's own `svg` or `img` the icon size too, and List.Item's `start` gives envUI's own symbols there the leading icon size, `--env-icon-size-lg`, in place of their text's. TopBar's `start` sets a small picture's size and PageHeader's a medium one's, for a Graphic of the icon size and an application's `svg` or `img` alike; envUI's symbols and icon buttons there keep their sizes.

  The picture sizes are tokens, `--env-picture-size-sm`, `-md`, `-lg` and `-xl`, 1.5, 2, 3 and 5.5 rem in both scales, which Avatar, AvatarGroup and Graphic take.

- One `HeadingLevel` type, `1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6`, types every `headingLevel`, in place of `Accordion.AccordionHeadingLevel`, `CardHeadingLevel` and `TableOfContentsHeadingLevel`; Accordion and Drawer take `Exclude<HeadingLevel, 1>`.
- IconButton can be named by `aria-labelledby`, the id of visible text that names its action, in place of `aria-label`; one of the two is still required.
- Icons take `tone`: `primary`, `accent`, `success`, `warning`, `danger` or `done` colors the icon with the tone's text color, `--env-color-fg-{tone}`, such as a run step that is done or failed; `neutral`, the default, keeps the color of the text around it. `IconTone` is its type. See https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/icons.md.
- `isPlaceholder()` is a type guard: `isPlaceholder(item) ? <Badge>…</Badge> : item.name` reads the loaded item's own `name`, a `string` rather than `string | undefined`, also where the items' ids are strings. A `Placeholder` comes only from `placeholders()`, so an object of the application's own no longer passes for one.
- The light theme takes a nearly neutral gray and orders its surfaces as the dark theme does: the page is the deepest surface (`--env-color-bg-page`, `#eaeaec`), panes, the navigation and sheets nearly white (`bg-default`, `#f6f6f8`), and tables, docked panels, cards, list rows, dialogs and popups white (`bg-raised`, `bg-muted`, `color.list-item.bg` and `bg-popup`, `#ffffff`), so what stands on the page lifts off it; frosted popups keep their translucency and soft shadow, which keeps their edge over a white card. The recessed surface is a shade lighter than the page and the panes (`#f8f8fa`), and accent text and links are a grade deeper (`fg-accent` and `fg-link`, `#0053b5`); text, edges and hairlines keep their grades in the new gray. At high contrast the page is `#f1f1f3`, surfaces and the recessed surface `#f8f8fa`, and cards and list rows white. No token is added or renamed, and the dark theme is unchanged. An application that set its own colors against the light surfaces checks them on the gray page.
- New: `LineArt`, a geometric drawing in hairlines for a banner's end, an empty state or the background of a header, such as a golden rectangle cut into squares or a chain of circles between three arcs. `drawing` names one of sixteen, listed in order as `lineArtDrawings`, such as `golden-section` or `harmonic-division`. It fills its container's box, covering it: `align` is `end` (the default), `start` or `center`, where the drawing sits as the box crops it, mirrored in right-to-left text; it always rests on the box's foot, cropped from its top. Its lines are the muted edge color, `--env-color-border-muted`, a hairline at any size, and `GrayText` in forced colors. It is hidden from assistive technology, or with `label` an image named by it. The root is `env-line-art`, an SVG with `data-drawing` and `data-align`. With `motion`, its lines draw themselves one beat after another in the order of the drawing's construction: `appear` draws them in, `disappear` draws them out, `loop` does both by turns, each turn beginning where the last one ends, and `play` runs each line out and in again while the drawing stays whole, ending on the still. `onMotionEnd` is called when `appear`, `disappear` or `play` finishes. A motion takes `--env-line-art-duration`, ten of `--env-motion-duration-slow` by default; it moves only the lines' dashes, so they stay hairlines and nothing around them moves. Where reduced motion is preferred, a motion shows where it ends at once, and a loop stays whole.
- List.Item's `selected` is `current`: it marks the current row, such as the one whose details show beside the list, with `aria-current`, while `selected` means chosen on Table.Row and Avatar.
- Menu.Root's `aria-label` is optional: the menu takes its trigger's name. Menu.RadioGroup takes `defaultValue` and Menu.CheckboxItem `defaultChecked`, so a choice can stay the menu's own. Menu.Separator, Menu.Group and Menu.RadioGroup pass their native props on, and the `onOpenChange` of Menu.Root, Menu.Submenu and ContextMenu receives why, `Menu.OpenChangeDetails`.
- Menu.Item takes `tone="danger"` for a destructive action, such as Delete: its label and icon take the danger color, while its row highlights as any other's, and the item carries `data-tone`. A count at an item's end, a Badge with `count`, shows as its figures alone, in its tone's color without the pill, so it can't be taken for a shortcut's keys. An unavailable item dims its label, icon and end, while its description, which says why, keeps the muted text color and stays readable.
- A message whose number picks a plural form receives `count`, the number, and `countText`, the count in the locale's digits. CommandPalette's `messages.commandPalette.results` and the toasts' `messages.toast.hidden` take `countText` too, and write their counts in the locale's digits; AvatarGroup's `number` is `countText`, and DiffStat's `added` and `removed` are `additionsText` and `deletionsText`.
- Message keys take the names of the props that override them: PanelDock's `messages.panelDock.show`, `hidden` and `closePanel` are `open`, `minimized` and `close`, after `openLabel`, `minimizedLabel` and `closeLabel`, and its header's Close control is `closeWindow`; DocumentTabs' and Tree's `actions` are `contextMenu`, after `contextMenuLabel`; TopBar's `loadingPage` is `loading`, after `loadingLabel`.
- MessageList's busy row names who replies and says what happens: `busyAuthor`, such as Agent, shows as a message's author, then the spinner with Working in the muted color, from `busyLabel` or EnvUIProvider's new `messages.message.working`, rather than a spinner alone.
- While `busy`, MessageList shows a quiet spinner in the muted color after the last message, where the reply will show, `env-message-list-busy`; it stays hidden from assistive technology, which reads `aria-busy`.
- Message namespaces are named after the component or concept they serve: CopyButton's `copy` messages are `copyButton`, and `themeToggle`, which ThemeToggle and ThemeMenuRadioGroup share, is `themePreference`. `common.clear` is `combobox.clear`, as only Combobox reads it, and the unused `common.more` is gone; `common` holds `loading`.
- TextField's notch closes at the pace its floated label settles, so the edge never crosses the label, and the pickers' fields keep their focused frame and label, `data-popup-open` on `env-date-picker-control`, until the popup has closed.
- SegmentedControl: one thumb, `env-segmented-control-thumb`, travels to the chosen segment, rather than two segments cross-fading their fills; under reduced motion and forced colors the chosen segment still draws its own.
- Tabs: one indicator, `env-tabs-indicator`, slides and resizes to the selected tab, where each tab used to draw its own; the outgoing panel goes at once and the new one fades in, so two panels never show over each other. Under reduced motion the indicator moves at once.
- Bar, Rail and Drawer are NavigationBar, NavigationRail and NavigationDrawer, with `NavigationBarProps`, `NavigationRailProps`, `NavigationDrawerProps` and `NavigationDrawerDestination`, after Material's navigation bar, rail and drawer: elsewhere a drawer is a panel over the page, and Bar named nothing in particular. Their part classes and color properties take the same names, `env-navigation-bar-*`, `env-navigation-rail-*` and `env-navigation-drawer-*` where they were `env-bar-*`, `env-rail-*` and `env-drawer-*`, and their style sheets are `NavigationBar.css`, `NavigationRail.css` and `NavigationDrawer.css`. The documentation page is `navigation-drawer`; `drawer` opens it.
- The `@envsemble/envui/icons` barrel is gone: import each icon by its own path, such as `@envsemble/envui/icons/Add`, which keeps a bundler from reading every icon.
- An outline Button, IconButton and Select trigger is just an edge: it has no fill at rest, so it reads the same on the page, a pane, a card or a popup, where it took the raised surface's fill before. Hover, press and an open Select lay the neutral state layer over the surface beneath it, as a ghost button does; the edge, the label's color and the danger tone are unchanged. A container or content color set on one button still applies as before.
- CommandPalette's `onOpenChange` receives why it closes as its second argument, `CommandPaletteOpenChangeDetails`, as Dialog's does: a dialog's reason, such as `escape-key`, or `item-press` when a chosen option closes it. `HoverCardOpenChangeDetails` names what HoverCard's `onOpenChange` receives.
- Pager.Root takes its links as Pager.Previous and Pager.Next children alone: its `previous` and `next` props are gone, so there is one way to write a pager.
- PanelDock's sizes are in rem, as Sheet's, NavigationDrawer's and Panes' are: a panel's `height`, `preferredWidth`, `minWidth`, `maxWidth`, `minHeight` and `maxHeight`, and the height `onHeightChange` reports, were pixels; divide them by 16, so `{ minWidth: 320 }` is `{ minWidth: 20 }`. A number for `stackBelow`, on PanelDock and Table, is rem too, where it was pixels; a CSS length works as before.
- Panes' types drop the namespace's name: `Panes.PanesBehavior` and `Panes.PanesSplitAt`, also exported as `PanesBehavior` and `PanesSplitAt`, are `Panes.Behavior` and `Panes.SplitAt`.
- Panes takes the words Sheet takes for one resizable width: `size`, `defaultSize`, `onSizeChange`, `minSize`, `maxSize` and `persistedSize` are `width`, `defaultWidth`, `onWidthChange`, `minWidth`, `maxWidth` and `persistedWidth`, and `readPanesSize` is `readPanesWidth`. `ResizeDetails`, `{ committed }`, is what every resize callback receives, Table's `onColumnWidthsChange` now too.

  Panes.Root takes `defaultOpen`, so a supporting pane opens from Panes.Trigger and closes without the application keeping its state, and `onOpenChange` receives why, `Panes.OpenChangeDetails`: `trigger-press`, `escape-key`, `close-press`, `outside-press` or `back-press`. The part props are `Panes.MainProps`, `Panes.DetailProps` and `Panes.ListProps`, for `PaneProps` and `NamedPaneProps`.

- Each component brings its own styles: its module imports its style sheet, after the base every component shares (the layer order, tokens, theme and font), so a bundler such as Vite or webpack puts in the page only the CSS of the components the application uses. A small application with Theme, Button, TextField and Card loads about 13 kB of CSS (gzip) instead of 49 kB, and a typical one with 23 components about 26 kB. In Node, as for server rendering and tests without a bundler, the same modules load without their CSS: the package's style sheet imports resolve to an empty module under the `node` condition, and to the CSS under the `module` condition bundlers set. `styles.css` still holds every style, for applications without a bundler.

  Migration: remove the `@envsemble/envui/styles.css` import when you use a bundler; importing it as well loads the styles twice. An application that declares cascade layers declares their order in a style sheet imported before any component.

- MarkdownEditor's and RichTextEditor's `onRequestLink` is `pickLink`: it returns the link the application's picker chose, rather than reporting an event.
- Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker pass `onOpenChange` a second argument, why the popup opens or closes, as the overlays do: `ComboboxOpenChangeDetails`, whose `reason` is one of the combobox's, such as `input-change` or `item-press`, and `PickerOpenChangeDetails`, whose `reason` is a popover's or `list-navigation`, `item-press` or `clear-press`.
- DatePicker's `defaultTime`, the time a newly chosen day starts with, is `placeholderTime`, as React Aria names it: `default*` names an uncontrolled starting value everywhere else.
- Every public custom property has a contract:

  - The token list, `src/generated/tokens.json`, also lists the hand-written tokens, `--env-radius-full`, `--env-viewport-gutter` and `--env-type-display`, marked `handwritten`, and `--env-line-height-body` is a token now, 1.4, which an application may set as before.
  - The part contract lists each component's other public custom properties under `properties`, with what each does and whether an application sets or reads it: Popup's `--env-popup-opacity`, `--env-popup-blur` and `--env-popup-shadow`, Tooltip's `--env-tooltip-opacity` and `--env-tooltip-shadow`, Scaffold's `--env-scaffold-sticky-top`, LineArt's `--env-line-art-duration`, IconButton's `--env-icon-button-hit-area` and TableOfContents' `--env-table-of-contents-depth`.
  - Every other custom property is private, such as the badge, count, key and diff stat hooks a control sets on what it holds, the avatar rings and `--env-table-of-contents-stroke`; the docs no longer name them.

- Success, information and danger toasts take the neutral toast's dark fill, `--env-color-bg-neutral-strong`, with their icon in the tone's light grade on a faint disc of it, rather than a solid green, blue or red fill, so a notification no longer outweighs everything on a busy screen. The icon colors are new tokens, `--env-color-fg-success-on-strong`, `--env-color-fg-info-on-strong` and `--env-color-fg-danger-on-strong`, at 3:1 on the disc, 4.5:1 at high contrast; a toast whose content color is set takes that color for its icon too. `--env-color-bg-{success,info,danger}-strong` stay. Every toast in a region is one width, 22 rem, less the gutters on a phone, so a stack's edges line up rather than following each message's length. The ESLint plugin and the Stylelint config accept the new tokens.
- `RangeBarProps`, the base of `MeterProps` and `ProgressProps`, is internal: type against `MeterProps` or `ProgressProps`.
- A recessed surface, `--env-color-bg-recessed`, with `-hover` and `-pressed`: a well on the page a shade deeper than it, in every theme, such as the stage of a preview, which what it holds rises out of. Everything measured on the page keeps its contrast on it, at standard and high contrast. Surface takes it as `background="recessed"`. The palette gains grade 3 in every hue, between the light page and the light panes.
- RichTextEditor's `ref` reaches its root element, as every component's does, and `editorRef` gives the `RichTextEditorHandle`, with the Tiptap editor.
- `@envsemble/envui/rich-text` exports `MarkdownLink` and `MarkdownLinkRequest`, the types of RichTextEditor's `pickLink`, so its users need no import from `/content`.
- RichTextEditor takes `minRows`, the lines of text its document is tall enough for while it holds fewer, such as `minRows={2}` for a short note in a card; without it the document is at least 10 rem tall, as before. See [RichText](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/rich-text.md).
- A rich text reference source's `char` is `trigger` and its `items` lookup is `search`, as `{ trigger: '@', label: 'People', search: findPeople }`, and a `RichTextReference`'s `char` is `trigger`. The Markdown an editor saved before reads as it did.
- Tree and table rows under the pointer take a state layer of their own, `--env-color-bg-row-hover` and `--env-color-bg-row-pressed`: a clear layer over the row's surface, in dark too, and a step below the selection, so the selected row always reads strongest. In dark, `--env-color-fg-link-on-muted` and its hover and pressed values are a grade lighter, so a link in a hovered or pressed row keeps 4.5:1. High contrast keeps its row states.
- Scaffold's `forms` is `navigationForms`, and the `NavigationForm` type is `ScaffoldNavigationForm`, so neither reads as a form of fields. `resolveNavigationForms` is internal: Scaffold works out which form shows at each window class itself.
- Scaffold's `skipLabel` and `skipNavigationLabel` are `skipToContentLabel` and `skipToNavigationLabel`, named after the messages they override, `skipToContent` and `skipToNavigation`.
- SearchField shows Search as its placeholder while its label is hidden and it is given no `placeholder`, rather than an empty field with only its icon. The text is EnvUIProvider's new `messages.searchField.placeholder`.
- Select is an outlined field, as TextField, Combobox and DatePicker are: its label rests inside the well and floats into the outline, and the chevron sits at the end. `variant` is a field's, `outline`, the default, or `floating`, for a long form on a page; the Button variants `secondary` and `outline` are gone, and a Select with `variant="outline"` keeps working. A Select without a visible label, with `aria-label` or `showLabel={false}`, as in a toolbar, is as wide as its longest option. Select takes `open` and `onOpenChange`, which receives why, `readOnly` and `showLabel`; `className` reaches the field's root. The well is `env-select-control`, the chosen option `env-select-value`, the placeholder `env-select-placeholder`, the chevron's place `env-select-end` and the hidden option labels that size it `env-select-sizer`; `--env-select-container`, `--env-select-content` and `--env-select-outline` color the well, the value and the edge.
- Skeletons pulse instead of shimmering: every bar and shape breathes between a stronger and a fainter tone, 1.8 s a breath, in step across the page, and the skeleton's own surfaces, such as cards, surfaces, list rows, a table's header and one's own message bubble, pulse with them, more faintly. With a reduced-motion preference the pulse is slower and shallower, a change of tone only; in forced colors the bars stand still in the system's gray. A popup, dialog or sheet shown as a skeleton stops pulsing as it closes, so it closes with any motion preference. The shimmer's highlight token, `--env-color-skeleton-shine`, is gone, as nothing paints it any more. In the light theme at high contrast, `--env-color-bg-neutral-muted-hover` and `--env-color-bg-neutral-muted-pressed` are as strong as at standard contrast, so skeleton bars, switch tracks and secondary buttons under the pointer are no fainter there.
- Slider's `ref` reaches its native range input, the element that holds the value and takes focus, as every field's `ref` does, so a form library can register it; `inputRef` is gone, and `className`, `style` and the other native props still go to the root. DropZone keeps `inputRef` for its file picker, and says why.
- Bullets in formatted text (Markdown, RichText and RichTextEditor's document) are a small spark, four points with curved sides, in the text color, instead of a dot; one level in, a smaller spark in the muted text color instead of a ring. Numbered lists are unchanged.
- The Tabs indicator carries `data-placed` once it has measured the selected tab and shows, beside Base UI's `hidden` until then.
- A Tabs item takes a `label` and an `icon`, as SegmentedControl's items do: `{ value: 'summary', label: 'Summary', content: <Summary /> }`. `button` stays for a control of the application's own.
- A text button's press reads apart from its hover: pressed, a text button with a label lays a faint layer of its label's color behind it. A danger text button with a label darkens a step under the pointer and another while pressed, as the link blue does, from the new tokens `--env-color-fg-danger-hover` and `--env-color-fg-danger-pressed`, where it kept one red in every state; in a selected table row it keeps the danger color. In the light theme a pressed link or text button, `--env-color-fg-link-pressed`, is a step deeper than before, two from its hover. The ESLint plugin and the Stylelint config accept the new tokens.
- `TextFieldVariant`, `'outline' | 'floating' | 'ghost'`, names a text field's look, and Textarea's `variant` reads `'outline' | 'ghost'`.
- ThemeRadioGroup is ThemeMenuRadioGroup, with `ThemeMenuRadioGroupProps`: it is the theme choice as a menu's radio items, and works only inside a menu.
- `ThemePreferenceValue`, what `useThemePreference()` returns, is exported, so a wrapper can type it.
- ThemeToggle takes no `href`: it runs its own press, so as a link it did nothing. It is always a native button.
- TimePicker, and DatePicker with `granularity="minute"`, take `hourCycle`, `h12` or `h23`, which sets the clock of the shown time and the wheels whatever the locale's: `hourCycle="h23"` shows 18:00 and hours from 00 to 23 with no AM/PM wheel, also in English. The value stays on the 24-hour clock, as `HH:mm`.
- A Toast's tone icon stands on its own, in its tone's color, without the disc behind it, at a control's icon size, 20 px, and level with the first line of the message rather than with the middle of the toast, so a message of two or three lines starts beside its icon. The icon and the message sit together in a new part, `env-toast-content`.
- Toast.Provider's `visibleLimit` is `limit`, as Base UI's Toast.Provider names it.
- `Toast.Provider` and `Toast.ProviderProps` are `ToastProvider` and `ToastProviderProps`, flat like EnvUIProvider, LinkProvider and ThemePreferenceProvider; the `Toast` namespace is gone. Its parts keep their `env-toast-*` classes, which `parts.json` lists under ToastProvider.
- Alert, Badge and Toast lose their outlines. An Alert is filled with its tone's stronger tint, its title takes the tone's color as its icon does, and its corners are a card's, 1.25 rem; a soft Badge in the accent, success, warning or danger tone takes the same stronger tint with a semibold label; a Toast has no edge and takes the alert's corners. At high contrast an Alert keeps the tone's softer tint with an edge in the border color, a Badge the softer tint and a Toast its tone's edge; under forced colors the system paints their edges. `--env-alert-outline` and `--env-toast-outline` still draw an edge at every contrast.
- `Toolbar.Button` takes `tone="danger"` for an action that destroys, such as Delete, so an icon-only button keeps the red its labelled Button has. See [Toolbar](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/toolbar.md).
- Tooltip takes `open` and `onOpenChange`, which receives why, so an application can open one for a step of a tour, and `align`, `start`, `center` or `end`, as Popover and HoverCard do.
- Tree: a row's children grow from its end and fade in as it expands, and shrink and fade out as it collapses, as an accordion's panel does, while the rows below glide with them; until they have closed they sit in `env-tree-group`. Under a reduced-motion preference they show and go at once.
- A Tree row, `TreeItem`, is keyed by `value`, which the tree's `value` names, where it had `id`, and its native tooltip is `tooltip`, where it was `title`. `TreeActionOptions` is `TreeActionDetails`. Tree passes its native props on to its element.
- Typeahead keeps typed letters adding up for 750 ms, as Base UI's menus and selects do: Tree waited 600 ms and the time pickers' wheels a second.
- Select's items take a `description`, a muted second line read as the option's description, such as why it is unavailable. An unavailable option takes the disabled text color, `--env-color-fg-disabled`, on its label rather than fading the row, so the label still sits above its description, which keeps the muted color: unavailable and explanatory text don't look alike.
- `windowClassOf(inlineSize)` gives the window class of a width in CSS pixels, such as `useContainerSize`'s `inlineSize`, at the root font size, so code that renders by the window class needn't convert the rem of `windowClasses` itself. See [Adapting to width](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/responsive.md).

### Patch Changes

- An accent primary button, `variant="primary" tone="accent"`, is a pill like every other button, rather than having square corners; its fill is unchanged, and in a ButtonGroup it takes the group's corners.
- Actions take the colors of the notice they sit in, so no second accent competes with its tone. A toast's action is an outline button in the toast's text color, with its edge in that color and the dismiss button's hover and press fills, rather than a blue primary button on the green, red or blue fill. In an Alert, outline, ghost and text buttons among the actions take the alert's text color, an outline's edge the tone's color, and the tone's layers on hover and press, rather than the link blue; a secondary button keeps its fill.
- An Alert rests on its tone's tint, `--env-color-bg-{tone}-muted`, in every theme and contrast, rather than the tone's hover layer, `--env-color-bg-{tone}-subtle-hover`, which its dismiss button and actions still lay on hover. Alerts and toasts take the large corner of the radius scale, `--env-radius-lg`, which the compact scale tightens, rather than a fixed 1.25 rem, so a one-line alert or toast no longer reads as a pill.
- In light, an Alert's tint, `--env-color-bg-{tone}-muted`, lies over the white raised surface, `--env-color-bg-raised`, rather than over whatever is behind it, so info reads blue, success green and danger red on the gray page instead of slate, sage and dusty pink; dark is unchanged. The title takes the alert's text color, `--env-alert-content` or `--env-color-fg-default`, and the icon and the tint carry the tone; an outline action's edge takes the icon's color.
- The props' descriptions spell as American English does, center and behavior, as the props' values do.
- AnimatedNumber: the value it changed from leaves as the new one arrives at once, so the new value reads from the first frames; they roll a whole line inside the number's box, and where the new value is wider or narrower the box eases to its width, so the words after it move rather than jump.
- In the dark theme an avatar takes a deep fill of its hue with light initials and figure, rather than the light theme's pastel, so avatars no longer outshine everything else on a dark screen; at high contrast too, at 7:1. In a table's cell and a small AvatarGroup the fill still takes half its chroma. See [Avatar](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/avatar.md).
- Avatars stay quiet in dense places: a small avatar and one in a table's cell cast no shadow, and in a table's cell and in a small AvatarGroup the fills take half their chroma at the same lightness, so the initials keep their contrast, and a small group, or one in a table, overlaps less, initials only by the ring that parts them, so their letters no longer touch. In an AvatarGroup, an avatar whose color looks like the one before it, such as two pinks, takes the next color that doesn't. See [Avatar](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/avatar.md).
- AvatarGroup keeps initials readable at rest: a group whose avatars show initials piles looser, so two letters stay whole under the next avatar, and a group of initials or a small group, as in a table's row, rests upright and in line rather than turned. The count of the rest is a flat circle the size of the avatars, which a larger count widens into a pill, rather than a shadowed rounded square.
- Avatar's initials stay legible at small sizes: at least the caption size, rather than 0.38 of the avatar, which fell to about 9 px at `sm`. An avatar smaller than `sm`, such as one sized to a dense row, shows its color alone, without initials.
- An avatar and AvatarGroup's count cast a faint, small shadow, 1 px down and 2 px soft, in a deeper shade of the surface below them, stronger on a dark surface than on a light one, so the avatars of a group part from the one they lie on. It is envUI's one drop shadow; `--env-avatar-group-ring` sets the surface it follows, it is gone under forced colors, and a chosen avatar keeps its rings alone. The hairline inside an avatar's edge is gone: the shadow holds its circle. At rest a group's pile is looser, each avatar showing about half of itself.
- A `done` Badge leads with a check mark unless it is given another `icon`, or `icon={null}` for none, and a soft one takes half its tint, fainter than a neutral badge, so completed work reads apart from neutral and primary. On a selected Table row or List item, a soft badge lays its tint over the raised surface, so it no longer sinks into the selection's blue.
- envUI now depends on Base UI 1.9.0, up from 1.8.0, which fixes focus, animation and touch handling across its components.
- ButtonGroup's buttons meet with a hairline between them and small inner corners, `--env-radius-xs`, so the group reads as one control with pill ends rather than buttons side by side; outline buttons in a group share their edge. It joins buttons of one variant, and filled primary and secondary buttons together, such as a split button; a group that mixes an outline, ghost or text button with another variant, such as an outline Cancel beside a primary Save, keeps them apart as whole pills a small gap from each other.
- In a narrow ButtonGroup a long label may break inside a word again, rather than push the group past its container: connected buttons can't wrap onto a line of their own.
- A vertical ButtonGroup of outline buttons reads as one framed control: the edges its buttons share run straight across, and only the column's outer corners are round, where small inner corners notched them into a stack of separate pills.
- A Button's label keeps to one line and the button to its label's width, so a row short of room wraps whole buttons instead of breaking a label such as Add filter over two lines: give such a row `wrap`. A `fullWidth` button and the buttons of a ButtonGroup, which can't move to a line of their own, still wrap their labels. See [Button](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/button.md#adapting-to-width).
- A pending Button's spinner turns in the button's label color again, rather than the muted text color, which made it nearly invisible on a primary fill.
- Button keeps a label's words and a number in their line together, such as Show, an AnimatedNumber and tasks: they are one run of text, `env-button-text`, spaced by word spaces, where the button's gap once stood between each of them as a wide space. Words with an inline element, such as `<strong>`, in their line stay together too; a Badge, a DiffStat or an icon is still an item of its own, the gap apart.
- Buttons read as available or not at a glance. A secondary button's label is in the text color, so it no longer looks disabled on a grey or tinted surface, and a secondary button among an Alert's actions takes the tone's deeper tint. An unavailable primary button, in any tone, takes the neutral fill and label rather than a paler brand color, and an unavailable outline or text button the neutral label.
- A Calendar, on its own or in a DatePicker's popup, names the weekdays one way at every width and scale: by their first two letters, Mo Tu We in English, where those tell the seven apart, else by the locale's narrow names where those do, so the names never run into each other in the narrowest days. Each column's header still carries the full name for assistive technology.
- A Calendar's days, on their own and in a DatePicker's popup, are a little less than a row height wide, 24 px at the compact scale and 32 px at the comfortable one, rather than a row height, so the columns stand close around the small numbers; the days of a week still touch, with a hair between the weeks, and touch screens keep touch-sized days. The time wheels of TimePicker and DatePicker keep a day's height, so a chosen day and time still look alike.
- The month buttons of a Calendar, and of a DatePicker's calendar, and MonthPicker's year buttons show larger arrows, 1.25 rem, on slightly smaller buttons, so the month's name, such as November 2026, stays on one line at both scales, and their header keeps `--env-space-1` under it before the weekdays or the months, so the header reads beside the field at the compact scale too.
- The days of a Calendar or a DatePicker outside `min` and `max`, and the months of a MonthPicker outside them, take the disabled text color, `--env-color-fg-disabled`, as well as fading, rather than fading the text color alone, which left them only a little lighter than the days that can be chosen. Today, or this month, keeps its accent color, faded.
- Checkbox: the box fills and empties with its mark, from the first frame, rather than at once, so the mark's fade shows on the fill as the box is cleared.
- Beside a label of two lines, a label that wraps or one with a description, the box of a Checkbox, the track of a Switch and the radio of a Radio sit on the middle of the label's first line rather than across both lines; a one-line label keeps them where they were. The rows of a Menu, a Select and a Combobox with a description put their check mark, icon, end content and shortcut on the label's line too.
- Checkbox and Switch answer the pointer with a halo: under it and while pressed, the shared state layer, `--env-color-bg-neutral-subtle-pressed`, rings the box or the switch's track, checked or not, and lays itself twice while pressed, a step stronger. The halo takes no room. An unchecked box's edge also darkens to the text color, `--env-color-fg-default`, and the box stays empty, so it doesn't read as a checked box that is unavailable; a switch that is off deepens its track a step as before and its ring darkens to the muted text color, `--env-color-fg-muted`. Checked boxes and switches that are on keep their hover and pressed fills.
- DatePicker's days and MonthPicker's months take the chosen fill and its white text at once, and a day or month the choice leaves loses both at once, so its text is never white on a fill half gone and two never read as chosen together; the hover fill still eases in and out.
- The warning tints are a clean light amber in light, where the warning text color at a low opacity looked tan, and the tone tints of alerts and soft badges are a little stronger in dark, where they nearly disappeared.
- Quiet buttons show their states more clearly: a text button's label is underlined under the pointer and while pressed, as a link's is; a ghost button with a label lays a lighter layer on hover than a secondary button's fill at rest; the pressed state layer of ghost and outline buttons, rows and tabs is a step stronger than their hover; and a secondary button takes a larger step from hover to pressed.
- Code blocks are highlighted each in a task of its own once the highlighter is ready, so a page with many blocks answers input between them rather than pausing until all are done.
- Code, CodeBlock and CodeDiff take their code theme's colors from the nearest Theme boundary's style sheet rather than an inline style of their own, so a page rendered on the server shows code in the reader's theme before it hydrates, as the bootstrap script themes the root; once hydrated nothing changes. A CodeDiff still showing the theme before a switch keeps that theme's colors until the new one loads, as before. Code no longer needs a client component around it.
- In dark, CodeDiff tints its added and removed lines with less of the change color's saturation, so the syntax colors no longer compete with red and green fills; the line numbers' gutter keeps the full change color and carries the change. Every token keeps its contrast on the lines.
- A CodeDiff line that wraps hangs its continuation four characters in, so it no longer reads as a new line without a number.
- CodeBlock highlights its code a few lines at a time, giving the browser its turn in between, rather than all of a block in one task: a long block, or the first block a page highlights, no longer holds up the page while it is highlighted. The colors are the same.
- A highlighted CodeBlock's tokens carry their colors in both code themes, and the style sheet shows those of the nearest Theme: the same markup in either theme, so code highlighted on the server before the page hydrates shows the reader's theme's colors, and switching the theme no longer renders every token again.
- ColorPicker's error shows the error icon before its message, as the shared field error, `env-field-error`, does under every field.
- Combobox's leading avatars show their initials: in the options and as the chosen option's in the field, an avatar takes the small picture size, `--env-picture-size-sm`, rather than 1.25 rem, below the size Avatar shows initials from. Where initials don't fit, in a small field at the compact scale and in a chip, an avatar of initials is left out rather than shown as a blank disc of its color; an icon, a photo or a figure stays. A read-only Combobox keeps its chevron in the field's quiet icon color, and it doesn't answer the pointer, as a read-only Select's.
- A multiple Combobox stays one row tall while its chips fit there: beside chips, the input is as wide as its text and takes what the chips leave of the row, and moves to a row of its own only once its text no longer fits, rather than whenever less than 4 rem was left. Where the browser can't size an input to its text, it keeps the 4 rem.
- On touch screens, Combobox's wrapped rows of chips lie a small step apart (0.5rem) instead of a touch height apart, so a field with several chips no longer spreads them far down. Each chip's remove button reaches its neighbours' with a hit area as tall as a row and its gap (2.25rem, well past the 24 px minimum), so the hit areas of neighbouring rows meet without overlapping.
- A chosen Combobox option keeps its label while the options change, such as with a server search, whatever text its value holds; a value with a NUL character in it showed as the value itself.
- CommandPalette leaves out a key hint with Enter in its keys, such as Enter Run, while there is no option to choose: as results load and when nothing matches. Other hints and the `footer` stay.
- CommandPalette's search shows its focus as a hairline edge in the focus color while it holds the caret, in place of a 2 px ring that read as a second frame around the always-focused field.
- CommandPalette's search field shows the focus ring again while it holds the caret.
- CommandPalette's search is TextField's ghost variant at the large size, kept opaque on the frosted palette with its placeholder in view, so the two share one look: the caret takes the accent color, the field shows its focus by its fill rather than a ring of its own, and under forced colors it is outlined like every field. `env-command-palette-search` is the field's root, beside `env-field` and `env-text-field`, and the input takes `env-text-field-input` beside `env-command-palette-input`.
- CommandPalette's empty state is one muted line at an option's height, its title and then its description after a dot, rather than an icon on a muted circle over a centred title, so the palette keeps about its height as results come and go. It shows an icon, at the start of the line where an option's sits, only when `emptyIcon` gives one; the Search and SearchOff icons it showed by default are gone. While results load, a small spinner turns at the search field's end, whether or not results already show, rather than in place of the search icon or the empty state's icon.
- The compact scale's control heights are 28, 36 and 44 px for `sm`, `md` and `lg` (`--env-control-height-sm`, `-md` and `-lg`), up from 26, 32 and 38, so a labelled field and a button of one size share one height and `size` sets a step you can see. Every control takes them: buttons, fields, Select, Combobox, the pickers, SegmentedControl, Pagination, the rail of Tabs and the controls of toolbars. Menus and the lists of Select and Combobox keep their rows at 32 px, as dense as before. The comfortable scale is unchanged. See [Spacing & density](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/spacing.md#sizes-in-each-scale).
- Composer's send button shows the shared pending spinner while `pending` is set or an enclosing form's action is pending, rather than an unavailable arrow, so a send in progress reads as work under way; the field keeps its text and height and stays editable.
- A ContextMenu inside a contained PortalContainer, such as a preview of a screen, is part of the container as its other overlays are: it opens at the pointer or below the focused element without hiding the rest of the page from assistive technology, locking the page's scroll or moving the page, which a modal context menu did there.
- In a contained PortalContainer, a Combobox's text field and a DatePicker's, TimePicker's or MonthPicker's field close their popup as other overlays' triggers do: Esc in the field closes it, and so does focus leaving a Combobox's field, where both used to leave it open.
- A Menubar inside a contained PortalContainer, such as a preview of a screen, closes a menu as another of its menus opens in its place, on hover or with the arrow keys; the menu that was open stayed open beside the new one there.
- Panes' supporting pane, as a sheet inside a contained PortalContainer, such as a preview of a screen, belongs to the container as its other overlays do: it is not modal, so it no longer makes the rest of the page inert, takes focus, keeps Tab in itself or locks the page's scroll, which it did there.
- Notifications in a contained PortalContainer, such as a preview of a screen, arrive and leave with the motion live notifications have, and their time stops while the pointer rests on one, as it does on a live notification; they still leave the page's F6 alone.
- ToastProvider loads the notices it shows inside a contained `PortalContainer` only there, as the region renders, so an application whose toasts show in the page no longer downloads them.
- TableOfContents keeps its headings while a modal popup, such as a dialog or the command palette, is open. The popup hides the page behind it from assistive technology, and a heading change read during that time used to empty the list and hide it.
- TableOfContents marks the current section's label too, beside the shape: its strokes are drawn a little heavier, about a medium weight, keeping the regular weight's widths, so no label rewraps as the reader moves. Under the pointer a label takes the text color, a clear step past the quiet labels around it, and pressed, muted text's pressed color, where both were a gray barely apart from rest.
- TableOfContents gives only the current section's label the text color: the sections passed are as quiet as those still to come, so most of the list no longer looks current. Passed links still carry `data-passed`.
- TableOfContents, reading the headings of its container, reads them again only when a heading, its text or the headings in what is added or removed change, not on every change in the container: an animated part of the page no longer has it read the headings every few frames.
- TableOfContents' shape runs smoothly into its round ends: with the reader's place just short of the first or last entry, the shape no longer steps in to a smaller round end, which showed as a second peak.
- TableOfContents' bulge stays whole at the list's ends: at the first or last section it is drawn smaller and a little toward the middle, so it tapers before the shape ends instead of being cut off into a drip. The shape runs a little past the first and last sections, into room the list now leaves above and below them, `--env-table-of-contents-room`, `--env-space-4` by default.
- A ContextMenu in a contained PortalContainer, such as in a preview of an interface, stays where it opened on its region as the page scrolls, rather than at the same place in the window, where it moved off what it was opened on. A point given by `anchorPoint` while controlled is taken on the region too, from the moment it is given.
- Customize and extend says where color properties go: on a component's root, or on each item for a collection's `-item-` properties, which don't inherit from the collection.
- Ghost and outline buttons, and icon-only text buttons, in the danger tone lay a faint tint of the danger color on hover and press, rather than the neutral grey, so the whole button stays the destructive one under the pointer: `--env-color-bg-danger-subtle-hover` at 60 % on hover and in full on press.
- In dark, the butter, sage and oat avatar fills (`--env-color-bg-avatar-{butter,sage,oat}`) are a grade lighter, butter two, with paler initials (`--env-color-fg-avatar-{butter,sage,oat}`), so they read as clean gold, green and oat rather than muddy brown and olive with tinted initials; the initials keep 4.5:1.
- In dark, the backdrop behind modal dialogs, sheets, confirm dialogs and the command palette, `--env-color-bg-backdrop`, is black at 70 % rather than 50 %, so it sets the dark page back as clearly as the light backdrop does, where it barely dimmed it.
- In dark, a danger primary Button or IconButton, and a solid danger Badge, is a saturated mid red with a white label, `--env-color-bg-danger-emphasis` with `--env-color-fg-on-strong`, rather than a pale coral with a dark label: beside the soft blue and lilac primaries a destructive action is now the deepest, weightiest fill, apart by lightness as well as hue. It lightens a step on hover and, pressed, deepens a small step below rest, as the other dark primaries do; the high-contrast dark theme takes a deep red with a white label too. Light is unchanged.
- In dark, the primary and danger emphasis borders (`--env-color-border-{primary,danger}-emphasis-hover` and `-pressed`) deepen a little on hover and a little more on press, rather than jumping to a saturated blue or red.
- In dark, `--env-font-weight-medium` is 460 rather than 500: light text on a dark ground reads heavier, so headings and leading labels, such as a NavigationRail's, keep the weight they have in light. See [Typography](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/typography.md).
