- In dark, frosted popups take the default surface, `--env-color-bg-default` (#28292b), instead of the popup color, `--env-color-bg-popup` (#343638), still at 90 % over a blur with their shadow, so they part from the cards, tables and rows they open over: menus and submenus, Select and Combobox lists, the command palette and its search, popovers, hover cards, the DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker popups, ColorPickerButton's popover and rich text suggestions. Solid popups, at high contrast, with reduced transparency and under forced colors, take it too, as the approved design draws them; at high contrast their edge is a modal's, `--env-elevation-3-border`, the strongest, in both themes. The light theme keeps #f5f5f7, and dialogs and other surfaces that take `--env-color-bg-popup` keep it. No token changes.
- In the dark themes, raised and muted surfaces such as cards and tables sit one step below popups again, so a menu, popover or dialog lifts off the card it opens over.
- In dark, dialogs, menus, popovers, lists and the command palette take the popup color, `--env-color-bg-popup`, one step lighter than the cards and rows they open over, so they lift off them instead of sharing their fill.
- In dark, the primary and accent fills are one family of soft colors at the same lightness and strength: the accent, `--env-color-bg-accent-emphasis` and a solid accent badge's `--env-color-bg-accent-solid`, is a soft lilac rather than a vivid magenta. Each lightens a clear step on hover and, pressed, takes a small step deeper than rest rather than turning nearly white (`--env-color-bg-{primary,accent}-emphasis-hover` and `-pressed`, which the checked controls on the primary fill take too); its dark label keeps 4.5:1 in every state. The light purples the dark theme uses, from the accent's text to its tints, are softer with it.
- In dark, primary, accent and danger primary buttons, and the checked checkboxes, radios and switches that take the primary fill, lighten on hover rather than deepen, so their dark label or mark gains contrast; light still darkens. A danger primary, and a solid danger badge, take a redder fill in dark than the accent’s (`--env-color-bg-danger-emphasis`), so Delete reads as danger beside the accent's lilac rather than as a second pastel pink.
- In dark, the info, success, warning and danger tints take a deeper, more saturated grade of their hue, so an alert or a soft badge reads as blue, green, amber or red rather than olive or grey. A secondary button in an alert stands clear of its tint in both themes: a card's solid fill in light, and the tone's tint lifted toward the text color in dark.
- A DatePicker's calendar fills its card. Where the card is as wide as its field, such as in a narrow window, the weeks spread across the card and the month buttons end at its edge, above Clear, rather than the calendar standing narrow in the middle with empty sides. The card is still no wider than the calendar's seven days or its field.
- A DatePicker with `granularity="minute"` keeps its time wheels whole: beside the calendar where the card has room, and under it, centred, where the room beside the field is too narrow for both, rather than running past the card's edge and cutting off the chosen minutes. The calendar is unchanged.
- `DescriptionList`: stacked, the pairs stand further apart than a label from its value, so each pair reads as one; `layout="auto"` stacks below 18 rem of the list's width rather than 24 rem, so a 360 px panel keeps labels beside their values.
- Dev mode counts what an element paints as it shows: a background of an opaque color whose blue is 0, such as `rgb(255, 0, 0)`, paints, and a border of a transparent color paints nothing. The `layout-paints` check and the 3D view's surfaces follow.
- Dev mode tells when a sticky element is stuck at the bottom or the right edge, of the page as of a scroll container; it measured those edges from the wrong line, so it said such an element was stuck when it wasn't, or never said so.
- The backdrop behind dialogs and the command palette is lighter in light, where it turned the page mid-gray, and a real darkening in dark, where the page color at half opacity hardly showed: `--env-color-bg-backdrop` is the dark page color at 32% in light and black at 50% in dark, and the lighter `--env-color-bg-backdrop-muted` behind a sheet 20% and black at 30%. A dialog in dark has the popups' faint hairline edge, so it stands clear of the darkened page.

  Where dialogs, sheets and the command palette are solid, with reduced transparency, their light edge, `--env-elevation-3-border`, is the default border's gray, so it still stands out against the lighter backdrop.

- Dialog and ConfirmDialog keep one gap under the title: the content follows the heading block at the gap between the title and the description, a little wider than before, rather than at the larger gap between the dialog's sections.
- DiffStat leaves out a side that is 0, so a change that only adds reads +40 rather than +40 −0; with nothing on either side it still shows both.
- Disclosure and Accordion show hover and press: a Disclosure's summary and an Accordion's heading lay the shared state layer, `--env-color-bg-neutral-subtle-hover` and `-pressed`, where they looked as at rest. A Disclosure's summary takes it in a small rounded rectangle a little past its words, its margin taking that back, and its focus ring takes those rounded corners rather than square ones; an Accordion's heading takes it inset from the hairlines, with its ring tracing it, and its title and panel sit 8 px in from the hairlines to match.
- A Disclosure's chevron is the icon set's chevron, `env-disclosure-indicator`, at the small icon size, `--env-icon-size-sm`, in the middle of the summary's first line, as a Tree's is, rather than the text character `›`, which sat smaller, lighter and off its middle once turned; it turns to point down while open, and the panel lines up with the summary's words.
- The packaged docs say each thing once. A component's page ends with its generated part names, color properties and built-in strings, which its Customize and its prose no longer repeat. `docs/migration.md` is a short guide to moving an app onto envUI. The For agents page is gone: setup's Lint rules and Coding agents sections, and the setup skill, hold what it said. A page's status is `alpha`, `beta`, `stable` or `deprecated`, where `alpha` was `experimental`, and Dev mode is alpha. The pages on developing envUI itself are no longer in the package.
- DocumentTabs' active tab takes the surface of the content below it, the envUI surface around the strip or the canvas, so it joins its document, with the selection-blue indicator along its bottom edge; the grey fill it shared with a hovered tab now marks hover alone.
- The `done` tone, a Badge's color for completed work, is a quiet slate blue-gray of its own rather than a violet or a cyan: completed things recede, and a done badge stays apart from an accent one, a success one and a primary one at table size. `--env-color-fg-done`, `--env-color-bg-done-emphasis`, `--env-color-bg-done-muted` and `--env-color-border-done` change in every theme, at the same contrast.
- A drag follows the pointer on the content when a box around it scrolls, the page among them, not only the nearest one: a List or Table row lifted by its handle stays under the pointer, and a TextField's resize edge, a Table column's boundary and the resize handles of Panes, Sheet, NavigationDrawer and PanelDock move as far as the pointer moved on the content, rather than as far as it moved in the window.
- A NavigationDrawer destination takes a `badge`: a short word at the end of its item, after the label and before any count, such as a Badge that says the page is New. It is part of the item's name. NavigationRail and NavigationBar leave it out.
- A resizable NavigationDrawer finds the labels cut short, which show in full in a tooltip, once the page is laid out, rather than laying the page out again for each label as it mounts: a long drawer no longer holds up the page as it renders or hydrates.
- NavigationDrawer's nested destinations under a section with an icon sit in past it, so their labels start a step inside the section's label rather than before it.
- A resizable NavigationDrawer renders only itself and its handle on each move of a drag, not its destinations, so dragging the edge of a long drawer no longer lags. Where the application keeps the width, keep `destinations` the same array while it changes, such as with `useMemo`, as the [NavigationDrawer page](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/navigation-drawer.md) shows.
- The formatting toolbar of RichTextEditor and MarkdownEditor wraps its groups onto more rows where it is wider than the editor, rather than scrolling sideways with Undo and Redo out of view; a row never starts or ends with a separator.
- Interaction states step evenly: a ghost button presses to its hover layer laid twice, so its press stands apart from its hover; the neutral fill of secondary buttons hovers a clearer step from rest; and a primary action presses one grade past its hover rather than two.
- In light, the success and danger tints (`--env-color-bg-success-muted` and `--env-color-bg-danger-muted`) are a light green and a light red rather than the tone's text color made translucent, which lay at the page's own lightness: a success or danger Alert and a soft Badge now part from the page as clearly as the information and warning ones.
- A field's two end actions, the clear button before a Combobox's chevron or a DatePicker's, TimePicker's or MonthPicker's icon, stand `--env-space-1` apart, so they no longer crowd each other in a small field. Beside a multiple Combobox's chips, which each have a cross, the clear button shows under a fine pointer only while the field is under it or has focus, keeping its room so nothing moves, and always under a coarse pointer; it stays in the accessibility tree and in reach of the keyboard. A chip's cross is a little smaller, 0.75 rem, so it reads as the chip's.
- The icon at the end of a Select, DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker no longer turns the accent color while the field has focus, as a Combobox's chevron never did: the field's frame shows focus, and the chevron or the calendar keeps its color.
- A field's floated label takes a control label's size, `--env-font-size-label`, rather than the small body size: 13 px at the compact scale rather than 12 px, so it reads as easily as the value, and 14 px, as before, at the comfortable scale. This applies to TextField, SearchField, Textarea, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker.
- A single-line field that shows its label is at least the md control height: a small one takes the medium height, so the floated label in the edge and the value below it no longer crowd each other, and at md and lg it is as tall as the buttons of its size. A field with its label hidden, as a search in a dense toolbar, keeps the small height and lines up with small buttons. Stacked in a Column or a Grid, a single-line field with a visible label keeps `--env-space-1` above it for the label, so it doesn't crowd the field above. This applies to TextField, SearchField, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker; [TextField's sizes](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/text-field.md#sizes) say how.
- A floated field label's notch starts where the field's rounded corner has flattened, so the outline runs level into it rather than ending in a small hook. On a pill-shaped field, the floated label sits just past the curve. This applies to TextField, SearchField, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker.
- The DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker popups and Combobox's list open 8 px clear of the field's box, as every popup opens from its trigger, rather than 4 px from it, where they landed on the field's description. The pickers' popups are placed against the field's box rather than the button inside it.
- `size="sm"` on a TextField, SearchField, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker or MonthPicker is for a field with its label hidden, as in a toolbar or a filter row. A small field that shows its label still takes the medium height, and now says so in the console while developing, so the size no longer seems to do nothing. See [TextField's sizes](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/text-field.md#sizes).
- Fields speak one state language: under the pointer an outline field's edge darkens toward its text, to the muted text color, rather than turning the accent blue, so the accent shows focus alone. This applies to TextField, SearchField, Textarea, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker, MonthPicker, Field.Control, ColorPickerButton and Composer. A focused ghost field draws the outline field's accent frame, twice as thick, rather than the fill it shows under the pointer, so its focus shows without a label too; the command palette's search keeps its own focus ring. `--env-text-field-indicator` colors the edge while pressed and focused, and no longer under the pointer, where the edge follows `--env-text-field-outline` and `--env-text-field-content`.
- Primary and secondary buttons set their labels in the medium weight, `--env-font-weight-medium`, so their few words hold their own on the fill; outline, ghost and text buttons stay regular.
- Button and IconButton: a ghost button's press eases in and out as the other variants' do, in the same color. Buttons, a checkbox's halo, a tree's rows and a disclosure's summary take their hover and press colors from the first frame, on the entering curve.
- CalendarHeatmap's tooltip closes without an error when the pointer leaves it for something outside the React tree, such as the page's background or the browser window.
- A large HoverCard or Popover, `width="lg"`, is at least 20 rem wide, the widest a medium one grows, so the two widths differ even when the content is short.
- Inline code is quieter in running text: the monospace carries it, on half the neutral muted fill, so a sentence with several identifiers no longer reads as a row of chips. High contrast keeps the whole fill. See [Code](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/code.md).
- Kbd's edge is the text color at 5 %, translucent like its fill, so a key looks the same on any surface: the page, a pane or a card, in light and dark. Its fixed gray edge disappeared on the light theme's gray page and stood out on lighter surfaces. At high contrast the edge takes the border color.

  Keys in a button's end slot, such as a search button's shortcut, are drawn the same way from the button's label color: a faint fill of it with an edge just stronger, in place of an outlined key on no fill.

- In a button's `end`, such as the search button's shortcut, the keys of a combination stand side by side with the + read but not shown, as in menus, popup lists and tooltips, so a shortcut reads the same everywhere but in running text.
- A RadioGroup's legend, a Field.Label and the label above a RichTextEditor or MarkdownEditor take the size, weight and muted color of a field's floated label, so the labels of one form read alike.
- LineArt's lines are fainter in the dark theme, about 1.4:1 on a surface as in light, where the muted edge stood out at 2.3:1. High contrast keeps the whole edge.
- An underlined Link, and a text Button with `underline`, rest on a lighter underline, the label's color at 40 %, and draw it in the full color under the pointer and while pressed, as the label darkens, so rest, hover and press read apart.
- A List row whose target is a Button keeps the button's label on one line where the row has room. The label wrapped to the width of the row's description, as the button's width cap counted it as no width in the row's text.
- A List's items set their row layout on their `li` themselves rather than through a Row component around each, so rendering a long list takes about a sixth less script time.
- A List loads the code that moves its rows only with `onReorder`, as it renders, so a page whose lists don't reorder no longer downloads it. A drag or key press on a handle before it has arrived waits for it and then moves the row as it would have.
- In an interactive List row, the keyboard focus ring of the `data-row-target` link or button traces the row, the area a press follows, rather than the label alone, whose ring cut into the description.
- List: an item's start slot is at least an icon wide, with its content centred, so a narrower mark, such as a running step's Spinner among icons, keeps its label in line with its siblings'.
- A disabled Menu item's label takes the disabled text color, `--env-color-fg-disabled`, as a disabled button's does, rather than the muted color of its description, so an unavailable command no longer reads like the explanatory text beneath it; the description stays muted and readable.
- A Menu group with a heading sits half the group space after the rows before it, and its heading closer to its rows, so a menu of a few labelled groups no longer runs into a tall column; a separator, such as the one before a destructive item, keeps the full space.
- A Menu.Header of plain words, such as Project, takes a group label's small muted style, so a menu shows one kind of heading; a header of richer content, such as the signed-in person's avatar, name and address, keeps its room.
- Menu keeps one column of check marks: in a menu where some radio or checkbox items have an icon and others don't, every check mark sits at the start and the labels still line up.
- A Menu's chosen radio item takes the selection's fill and text, `--env-color-bg-selected` and `--env-color-fg-on-selected`, with its check mark and icon in the same color and the selected hover and pressed fills, as a Select's chosen option and a RadioGroup's chosen row do. Menu.Item's color properties still win.
- Menu, ContextMenu and Menubar menus: a Menu.Separator, and the space before a group without a heading, is a break of half a row, rather than a quarter that read as uneven spacing between rows; it still draws no line. Groups with a heading keep their spacing.
- In Menu, ContextMenu, Menubar menus and the rows of CommandPalette, Select and Combobox, a shortcut, or a Kbd at a row's end, is muted text in one unit, such as Ctrl+D, rather than a filled chip for each key, so a list of shortcuts stays quiet down its rows; on a highlighted row it takes the text color. Kbd elsewhere, such as in a button's end, a tooltip or the command palette's key hints, keeps its key caps.
- A Menubar in an inert region, such as the page behind a modal dialog, takes no access keys from outside that region: holding Alt no longer underlines its letters, and Alt with a letter no longer opens its menus, while keys pressed inside the region still reach it.
- Meter's and Progress's track, and Progress's segments, take the neutral fill a Slider's track takes, `--env-color-bg-neutral-muted`, in place of a faint state-layer tint, so empty steps stay visible on dark panels.
- `styles.css` ships minified: 358 KB in place of 425 KB: without comments and whitespace, with the same rules, values and cascade layers.
- MonthPicker's grid names its months at one length where the locale makes nearly all of them that long, such as Sep rather than Sept among en-GB's three-letter months; names with punctuation or digits, and locales whose names vary in length, keep the locale's own. Each month's accessible name is unchanged.
- The chevrons of Select, Combobox and NavigationDrawer's sections turn half a circle as they open and close, rather than flipping over and squashing flat halfway.
- Checkbox's mark settles in from a little smaller and fades out as the box is cleared, and Switch's thumb changes its color with the track as it slides.
- Motion refinements: colors under the pointer change in `--env-motion-duration-fast` with `--env-motion-ease-enter` on Tabs' labels and TextField's edge and label; a Slider's fill and handle reach a value a press on the track chooses in `--env-motion-duration-fast`, and a pressed handle grows a little; an Accordion panel, a NavigationDrawer section and a Tree row's children fade over their whole closing, so no empty band is left; Select's and Combobox's chevron turns back as fast as the list closes; and a List row's link or button lays no pressed fill of its own inside its pressed row.
- `--env-motion-ease-exit` is a gentler acceleration, `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 1, 1)` rather than `cubic-bezier(0.3, 0, 0.8, 0.15)`, so popups, dialogs, sheets and notifications fade evenly through their exit instead of staying nearly opaque for its first half and then vanishing.
- What enters fades in with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, so it shows from its first frames rather than late: popups, dialogs, sheets, toasts, the command palette and a tab's panel. Menus, selects, popovers, hover cards and the pickers' popups grow from 98 % over `--env-motion-duration-medium`, rather than from 96 % over the slow duration; a Tooltip only fades, in `--env-motion-duration-fast`. A new tab's panel starts half faded, so switching tabs never shows an empty panel between the two. Leaving is unchanged.
- Disclosure, Tree, Accordion and NavigationDrawer's sections open and close alike: Disclosure's details grow and shrink as an accordion's panel does, where the browser can size them to their content, and stay until they have closed; a panel's content fades in and out as it grows and shrinks; the rows a Tree reveals fade in; every chevron turns at the same pace; and an accordion's plus and minus fade into each other.
- Hover and press feedback lands from the first frame: colors, state layers and the small reveals under the pointer change over `--env-motion-duration-fast` with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, rather than with `--env-motion-ease`, which starts slowly, in rows, menus, tabs, cards, the navigation, the pager, segmented controls, switches, radios, sliders, tables, fields' fills and the other controls.
- Slider: a press on the track or a key glides the fill and the handle to the value, while a drag still moves them at once, and a drag past the ends eases back without overshooting.
- All of envUI's motion follows the motion tokens, so setting `--env-motion-duration-fast`, `--env-motion-duration-medium` and `--env-motion-duration-slow` to `0s` on `.env-theme` switches it off, as [Customize and extend](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/customizing.md#motion) now shows with all six tokens. Notifications glide in the slow duration and a revealed one fades in the medium one, with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, and don't move with durations of `0s`. TopBar's loading bar creeps for forty slow durations, completes in the slow one with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, fades out in the fast one with `--env-motion-ease-exit`, and is removed once its fade has ended. A Spinner turns in six slow durations, a Skeleton pulses in seven and a half and an indeterminate Progress sweeps in five, about as fast as before, with `--env-motion-ease`.
- NavigationDrawer's group headings are semibold in the text color, and its destinations are muted, gaining contrast on hover and press, so each group stands apart from its destinations.
- Navigation items under the pointer take a cool gray in light, a step quieter than the selection, as in dark, rather than a blue-gray deeper than it that read as the current item (`--env-color-bg-navigation-hover` and `-pressed`). A selected item under the pointer still reads as selected.
- NavigationRail's and NavigationBar's indicators and NavigationDrawer's items are rounded rectangles with the medium corner, `--env-radius-md`, rather than Material's pills; the rail's and the bar's indicator is 2.75 rem wide around the icon. Their fills, focus rings and layout stay.
- A Sheet is on the navigation's background, `--env-color-bg-default`, rather than the popup's, in every theme, so navigation that opens in a sheet in a narrow window keeps the color it has at the window's edge in a wide one. The sheet keeps its elevation edge and scrim; Dialog, menus and popovers keep the popup's fill. Panes' supporting sheet takes the same fill, which differs from its former raised one only at high contrast in the light theme.
- `null` and `false` both show none, on Alert's `icon` and on CommandPalette's `emptyIcon` and `emptyDescription`, where `false` left an empty icon box before.
- A focused outline Button or IconButton shows one outline: its ring lies on its edge, which takes the focus color, rather than a blue ring a hair outside a gray or red edge. In dark, a focused primary button's ring takes the text color, `--env-color-fg-default`, so it stands apart from the soft blue fill of its own hue. An Alert's dismiss button draws its focus ring inside its circle, the size of its hover fill, rather than around it.
- Every tone's primary button steps the same way under the pointer and while pressed: in light, the danger primary darkens a grade on hover and another on press, as the default and accent primaries do, where it lightened; in dark, the soft primary, danger and accent fills deepen by a clear, even step on hover and again on press, where their hover barely showed (`--env-color-bg-{primary,danger,accent}-emphasis-hover` and `-pressed`). A destructive outline button's edge and an invalid field's border darken on hover and press too (`--env-color-border-danger-emphasis-hover` and `-pressed`).
- A Select, Combobox, DatePicker or TimePicker whose list or calendar is open keeps its focused frame, the accent edge and label, also while focus is in the popup, as when it opens under the pointer, so the field reads as the list's. A focused field under the pointer keeps the accent edge rather than the pointer's darker one.
- An outline Button and IconButton take the text color, `--env-color-fg-default`, for their label and icon, rather than the brand blue, so an outline Cancel beside a primary Rename reads as the other choice rather than a second blue action. A text button keeps the link blue, and a primary button the brand blue fill. Among an Alert's actions an outline button keeps the alert's text color.
- In a compact Page, the content follows the header at the gap between a page's sections rather than the wider gap a medium page keeps, so tabs under a header whose actions take their own row no longer sit far below them.
- Pager's links take the large corner of the radius scale, `--env-radius-lg`, rather than the small one.
- A PanelDock header takes the default surface (`--env-color-bg-default`) once the content scrolls beneath it, a step below the panel toward the page, where it took the muted surface, which now equals the panel's raised surface in both themes and left the scrolled header unmarked.
- `readPanesWidth`, and Panes under `persist`, treat a kept width that is no valid encoding, such as a cookie value with a stray `%`, as no width kept, rather than throwing.
- Panes' supporting pane, as a sheet below its split width, has no edge line, as a Sheet hasn't: its fill and its scrim set it apart, and the edge returns at high contrast.
- Panes ignores a `width`, `defaultWidth` or `persistedWidth` that is no number, such as NaN from a stored value that didn't parse, as Sheet and NavigationDrawer do: the pane keeps its width as if it weren't given.
- Dev mode's `PartsEntry` types `root` as `string | null`, as `parts.json` has it for a shared group without a root, so `installDevMode({ parts })` takes `@envsemble/envui/parts.json` as imported.
- The part contract, `docs/generated/parts.json`, lists more of each state attribute's values: those a component sets through its props, such as Text's `body` variant and TextField's `outline`, `floating` and `ghost`, and through an element's dataset, such as List's `keyboard` move, and `""` for the flags that are only present or absent.
- Every pending Button and IconButton takes one model: a spinner the size of an icon turns centred over the button, in the label's color, while the label and icons stay in place, transparent, so the button keeps its width, its centred label's place and its accessible name. This replaces a small spinner squeezed into the padding beside a visible label, which read as a stray mark and set the label off centre; an underlined text button drops its underline while pending.
- The calendar of a DatePicker, and a Calendar, take the approved Frosted design: the month's name leads the header in semibold body text, the two month buttons side by side at its end at 0.8 of the md control height; the days of a week touch, each a row height wide, with a hair between the weeks, under 1.75 rem weekday names in the caption size; today is in the accent color, semibold, rather than ringed, and a day under the pointer takes the neutral fill, as a popup's rows do. A MonthPicker's year header and months follow.
- The DatePicker's calendar card takes the width of a field a little wider than it, up to 1 rem, so it ends with the field, with the calendar in the middle and its days still touching; Today and Clear follow the calendar 4 px below, rather than 12 px. The calendar keeps six weeks, so its height holds from month to month.
- In a narrow window, the card that DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker open is at least as wide as its field, so it ends with the field rather than leaving a gap at its side; a calendar sits in the middle of it with its days still touching. From a medium window up, the card keeps the calendar's width beside a wide field.
- The check mark of a chosen option in a Select or Combobox list ends its row, after the label and any end content, as on the approved Frosted design, rather than leading it. Rows keep its slot whether or not they are chosen.
- Popups take a corner of their own, concentric with their rows, half a row's height (20 px, 16 px at the compact scale), rather than a surface's 28 px and 22 px, so a popup is only a step rounder than the field or button that opens it: popovers, hover cards, ColorPickerButton's popover and the DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker popups, as menus and the Select and Combobox lists already were. Dialogs, sheets and the command palette keep a surface's corner, and the frosted fill is unchanged.
- A popup, such as a Menu, a Popover or a Tooltip, reads its text direction as it first renders, so opening it renders it once rather than twice.
- A popup that held focus, such as a select's list, a menu, a popover or the calendar of a date picker, gives focus back to its trigger as it starts to close rather than once it has faded out, so a key pressed meanwhile, such as Enter to open it again, reaches the trigger instead of being lost in the closing popup. A date, time or month picker opened again while its popup still fades out shows it afresh, the calendar at the value with focus on the chosen day.
- In menus, Combobox lists and the command palette, a group's label sits 10 px from the popup's edge (a quarter of a row's height), a little ahead of its rows' icons and text, with a touch of letter spacing, as on the approved Frosted design, rather than in line with the rows' labels. The options of Select, Combobox, rich text suggestions and the command palette lie one under the next with no space between them, and the palette's options take the text color, `--env-color-fg-default`, rather than the controls'.
- In menus, popup lists (Select, Combobox, the command palette's options) and the command palette's hints, the keys of a combination are flat chips side by side, as on the approved Frosted design: the + between them is read but no longer shown, the chords of a sequence stand a little further apart, a shortcut keeps 1 rem from its label, and the palette's hints are in the small label size with their keys close to what they do.
- Popup lists mark each thing once: the option or item the pointer or the keyboard is on takes the neutral fill, with no focus ring of its own, while the chosen one in a Select, a Combobox or a menu's radio group keeps the selection's fill with its check mark. In dark, frosted popups take a faint hairline edge, so they keep their edge over a card of their own shade. The time wheels of DatePicker and TimePicker show the chosen time in the selection's tint rather than three solid pills.
- Popovers, hover cards, ColorPickerButton's popover and the DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker cards take a panel's inset, as on the approved Frosted design: 14 px (0.35 of a popup row's height, 11.2 px at the compact scale), as wide as a popup row's corner, in place of 1.5 rem on popovers and hover cards and 1 rem on the picker cards.
- Under reduced motion an indeterminate Progress shows faint stripes across the whole track, rather than a still bar partway along that read as a value.
- An indeterminate Progress sweeps at an even pace, so the bar enters the track as it leaves and the track never sits empty, which read as a stall; the period is the same.
- Every optional boolean prop states its default, and the labels, icons and week starts that wrote theirs in prose state it as a default, so the props tables show them; the props that had no description have one.
- Bold text in prose, Markdown's and a RichText document's, is semibold rather than medium, so it stands out from the regular body text around it.
- Markdown, RichText and RichTextEditor keep a list item's line its own height when it holds more than one key cap, such as <kbd>Ctrl</kbd>+<kbd>K</kbd>: only the blocks in an item or a quote stand a step apart now, so a second key, or another element that runs in the line, no longer takes their margin, which pushed the line, and the item's text, below its bullet.
- In Markdown and other `env-prose` text, a table's header cells line up at the start, over their columns' text, rather than in the middle.
- TextField's prefix and suffix are quieter than muted text, still 4.5:1, so a value such as `quarterly-report` reads apart from the `https://example.com/` before it.
- RadioGroup's error, `env-radio-group-error`, looks as a field's does, the error icon before the message.
- The chosen row of a RadioGroup with `orientation="vertical"` takes the selection's fill and text, `--env-color-bg-selected` and `--env-color-fg-on-selected`, with the selected hover and pressed fills, as a selected list row and Select's chosen option do, rather than the hover fill among the other rows. Its description stays muted; a Radio's own color properties still apply.
- In a vertical RadioGroup, the chosen row shows its choice with the selection's fill and the filled radio, and its label keeps the text color rather than taking the selection's. On a surface of the rows' own color, a dialog's, a popover's or a muted or raised card's or Surface's, the rows take a faint tint of the neutral fill, so they keep their shape.
- A NavigationRail item's keyboard focus ring rings its indicator, 2 px out, rather than drawing a square around the whole item at the window's edge.
- Rating: filled stars take a clear gold of their own, the new `--env-color-rating-star` token, in place of the warning tone's brown, so a half star reads clearly against the empty grey.
- A read-only field, a TextField, Textarea, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, TimePicker or MonthPicker, or a Field.Control, keeps its resting outline, which doesn't answer the pointer, rather than a disabled field's muted one, so it reads neither as waiting for changes nor as unavailable; its value keeps full contrast and its label its place, and a read-only Select, Combobox or picker keeps its chevron or calendar, muted, so it still reads as a choice. A focused field draws its outline twice as thick in the accent, all the way round the floated label, without moving anything, as do the color picker's value fields.
- RelativeTime's dotted underline, which marks the tooltip with the full date, shows only under the pointer and with keyboard focus, everywhere rather than only in a Table: a time reads as a time, and lists repeat them. Other text with more on hover, such as a Tooltip's or HoverCard's text trigger, keeps its dots at rest.
- Pages restyle less as they change and hydrate. AvatarGroup, Checkbox, Radio, Switch, Page, Table and Tabs no longer have the browser work out the style of the whole page again whenever anything on it changes, such as a node added or a section of a navigation drawer opening: their style rules name the parts they style rather than any child or a pseudo-element after a `:has()`. A label cut short in a NavigationDrawer, Breadcrumbs or a full-width SegmentedControl no longer renders its tooltip again as the page hydrates, and ThemePreferenceProvider reads the canvas color only where the page has a `theme-color` tag. Nothing looks different.
- A disabled RichTextEditor no longer fades as a whole: its document takes `--env-color-fg-disabled` and its outline the muted border, as a disabled TextField does, so it stays readable, also in dark.
- RichText shows a line break within a paragraph, a single newline in the Markdown, as a new line, and keeps runs of spaces, as RichTextEditor shows them, rather than joining the lines, so saved text reads as it was written, such as a line that starts after a reference.
- RichText: a task's row in RichText is as tall as in the editor, its text no longer pushed down; and the suggestions list scrolls to keep the active option in view as the arrow keys move past its edge, and closes as focus leaves the editor rather than on any press on the page.
- RichText and a read-only RichTextEditor show a task's checkbox read-only rather than disabled: in full color, with `aria-readonly`, out of the tab order, and a press doesn't change it, so finished text no longer looks greyed out; a disabled editor's tasks stay disabled. The checkbox sits on the middle of the first line of its text rather than a little above it.
- The row densities of Table and List differ by at least 4 px of row height in the compact scale, where `xs` and `sm` were 2 px apart: `--env-row-padding-block-xs` is 0 and `-sm` 2 px, and `md`, the default, and `lg` keep their heights; the comfortable scale is unchanged, and so are a Tree's rows. A zero size token keeps its unit, such as `0rem`, so it adds in a `calc()`.
- A Scaffold loads the code that scrolls its body with the keyboard only with `scroll="body"`, as the body mounts, so a page whose document scrolls no longer downloads it. A key pressed before it has arrived scrolls the body once it has.
- SegmentedControl: under the pointer a segment not chosen takes no layer, only the text color on its label, and pressed only the faint neutral pressed layer, `--env-color-bg-neutral-subtle-pressed`, with a muted label, so only the thumb reads as chosen and hover never looks like a second selection, in the dark theme too. In dark the thumb is the raised neutral grade, `--env-color-bg-neutral-strong-hover`, opaque and clearly lighter than the track and a hovered segment; at high contrast it keeps a layer of the neutral fill.
- A disabled Select's chevron takes the disabled text color, `--env-color-fg-disabled`, as its value and label do, rather than staying in the text color.
- Select's open list lines up with the field's outer edges: it is placed against the outlined field rather than the button inside it, and a field that fits its options leaves room for the list's check marks, so the list is no wider than the field.
- Select's open list is at least as wide as the field; when options have descriptions it grows up to 20 rem, or the field's width where that is more, and descriptions longer than that wrap, so a short description stays on one line beside a narrow field rather than wrapping to the field's width.
- A Select's list opens 8 px clear of its trigger, as every popup does, also at the compact scale and on a small trigger, where it opened as close as the trigger's own gap.
- `SelectOpenChangeDetails` types `reason` as the reasons a select gives, such as `'trigger-press'`, `'item-press'` or `'escape-key'`, as the other overlays' details do, rather than as any string.
- A checked or mixed Checkbox and a Switch that is on that are disabled take the neutral fill, `--env-color-bg-neutral-muted`, with their mark or thumb in `--env-color-fg-disabled`, rather than a faded primary, as a disabled button does; their labels still fade. A Checkbox with `aria-readonly` keeps its colors and doesn't answer the pointer.
- The [setup guide](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/setup.md)'s install commands name the package's exact version, so they run as copied.
- A side Sheet keeps its actions at its foot, as on the approved Frosted design, however little its body holds, rather than right under the content; a bottom sheet stays as tall as its content.
- A Sheet too short for all it holds scrolls its content, `env-sheet-body`, between its heading and its actions, which stay in view, where the whole sheet scrolled and its actions went out of view with the content.
- A Sheet, and a NavigationDrawer in one, is solid within `--env-motion-duration-fast` as it slides in, with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, rather than fading in over the medium duration, so a sheet as wide as a phone's window no longer shows the page through it as it opens; the backdrop keeps the slower fade.
- Button's props, the fields' `size`, TextField's `variant` and every component's `skeleton` are described in a sentence each, their rules left to the pages; `variant` no longer says a ghost field has no fill at rest, nor `tone` that an accent primary has 0.5 rem corners.
- In a skeleton only the bars and shapes pulse: its own surfaces, such as cards, surfaces, list rows and a table's header, keep a faint tint that barely moves, so they keep their edges against the page and the layout no longer breathes.
- Skeletons draw the bars over their text faster when many are on a page at once: every skeleton's text is measured before any skeleton's bars are set, so the page is laid out once rather than once for each skeleton.
- Pages load less of envUI's JavaScript:

  - The script that draws a skeleton's text as bars loads only once a skeleton shows, so a component that can be a skeleton, such as Alert, Popover or Menu, no longer brings it along.
  - An icon writes the path its filled and outlined styles share once: 1,803 icons are smaller.
  - Development reports, such as a Tooltip's around a child that takes no ref, and the checks behind them, are left out of production builds.
  - CodeDiff loads its code themes with its highlighter, not with the page: 30 KB less before a diff shows.

  Alert is 30 % smaller, and a typical application's share of envUI's own code is 5 % smaller.

- The theme rules of `styles.css` no longer repeat a token's value where the rules below already give it, such as a dark value that is the light one: 5 KB less, with every token computing as before.
- The spark that marks a bullet in formatted text ships as a local asset, `assets/spark.svg`, beside the font, instead of a `data:` URL in the stylesheet, so the stylesheet keeps to local assets only.
- Spinner's arc keeps a minimum length as it turns, rather than shrinking to a dot at the start of each turn, so a small spinner, such as a pending Button's, reads as an arc in every frame.
- The surfaces keep one order in every theme: the page and the recessed well lie below the default surface, and the filled and raised surfaces a step above it with one fill. In dark a raised surface (`--env-color-bg-raised`, tables, docked panels, the pager and `background="raised"`) is `#343638`, the filled surface's, rather than the default surface's shade, and in light at high contrast it is white, as cards are. In light the recessed surface (`--env-color-bg-recessed`) is `#f1f1f3`, between the page and the panes rather than lighter than both; it keeps the panes' fill at high contrast, where nothing fits between. The light selection (`--env-color-bg-selected`, with its hover and press) is a grade deeper, so a selected row still stands apart from a hovered one on the recessed surface. A page Card's action strip takes no band of its own, so the card no longer shows as its strip alone. See [Colors](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/foundations/colors.md).
- A Switch that is on and unavailable keeps its fill and its thumb, faded with its label, so it still reads as on, rather than a faint tint with a gray thumb, which read as off. A switch that is off keeps its muted thumb.
- A Switch that is off has a muted thumb, `--env-color-border-default-pressed`, at 3:1 on its track, rather than the near-black control color, so it no longer outweighs a switch that is on; under the pointer and while pressed it darkens to `--env-color-fg-muted` with the ring. A switch that is on and unavailable takes a faint tint of the primary, `--env-color-bg-primary-muted`, rather than the neutral fill, so it differs from an unavailable switch that is off by more than the thumb's place.
- A captioned Table names itself above its frame rather than inside it: the caption sits over the rounded, bordered rows like a heading, so the column header band is the table's only header. Stacked on a narrow screen, the caption stays above the framed rows too.
- In a Table, a cell's secondary line, `<Text variant="caption">`, keeps to one line and ends in an ellipsis where its column is narrower, rather than wrapping at a hyphen and making the rows uneven.
- A Table.Cell whose props are unchanged, such as one holding text, renders nothing again when its row renders, so a table whose rows an application renders anew changes only the cells whose values changed: changing one row of a 1,000-row table takes about a quarter less of React's time.
- Table: a selected row whose first cell holds a checkbox takes no bar at its start, since the checkbox and the selection fill already say it is selected; a row selected without one, such as the row open in a detail pane, keeps the bar.
- A Table's column resize handle takes Page Up and Page Down, which widen or narrow the column by 5 percent, as other resize handles do, and moves by exact steps from the column's width. A drag the handle loses its pointer in, as when the browser takes it over, returns the columns to where it began.
- `Table`: a sortable header's focus ring sits a hair inside the header, following its corners and, in the first and last headers, the frame's, so the table's rounded corners never cut it.
- In dark, a Table's header band and row dividers are a light step closer to its surface, in the same hues, so they weigh what they do in light; the band, the frame and the high-contrast themes are unchanged.
- A Table whose columns don't fit its region fades at the edge it scrolls toward, so a column cut short reads as more to scroll to; the fade is narrow, so it never dims the column it cuts.
- A sortable Table header's focus ring has a row's rounded corners, inside the cell, as every other ring does, where it was a square-cornered rectangle; the first and last headers keep the frame's outer corners.
- A sortable Table header under the pointer takes no fill, which outweighed a hovered row: its label darkens to the text color, with its arrow showing.
- TableOfContents' marker of the reader's place keeps its shape as it moves: it follows the scroll as it is, and glides calmly to a section further away in `--env-motion-duration-medium` with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, rather than trailing behind on springs that stretched, leaned and wobbled it as it landed. The current label's color changes in `--env-motion-duration-fast` with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, ahead of the marker, so only one label reads as current, and the swell under the pointer grows in `--env-motion-duration-fast` without popping past its size.
- TableOfContents marks the reader's place with one shape down the side of the list instead of a dash before each section, in one quiet color, `--env-color-bg-neutral-muted`: straight along the list's start and bulging broadly toward the labels beside the current section, thin a few sections away, and a little shorter in a short list; the labels alone tell the sections passed from those to come. It rests on each section and moves on to the next as the page scrolls, on a spring: it stretches and leans back as it travels and settles with a short wobble as it lands, then stays still; at once, without the wobble, where reduced motion is preferred. It swells a little under the pointer, where the section's label turns a quiet gray, and takes the system's text color in forced colors. It is `env-table-of-contents-wave`, an SVG hidden from assistive technology, `--env-table-of-contents-stroke` wide at its fullest, now `--env-space-5` by default, so the labels sit a little further in. Inside a transformed or zoomed container, such as a scaled preview, the current section is measured in the scrolling area's own pixels, where it was once marked early or late.
- In a Table, where links and times repeat down the rows, a link, such as a Link, takes the cell's text color, rather than the link blue that made a solid blue column, and shows its underline only under the pointer and with keyboard focus, and a RelativeTime with a tooltip its dotted line only then; in a selected row too, and text buttons that aren't links keep the link colors.
- A Table loads the code and the style sheet of its column resize handles only with `resizableColumns`, as it renders, so a page whose tables don't resize their columns no longer downloads them. The handles show once that code has arrived, right after the table.
- Table: a selected row takes a lighter tint of the selection fill, so wide selected rows no longer outweigh the names and states in them; it stays a step above a row under the pointer, and the checkbox or the start bar still marks it. At high contrast it keeps the full fill.
- Skeletons draw one placeholder per row: a Table skeleton's sortable header cells are bars like the other headers, rather than one dark slab; NavigationRail and NavigationBar skeletons leave out a count's badge over the icon, a NavigationDrawer skeleton its sections' toggles, and text a closed section clips away no longer draws a bar over the rows below it.
- A Table shown as its skeleton draws no selection: a selected row takes neither the selection fill nor its bar while the rows load.
- With a pointer that hovers, an unsorted sortable Table header shows its arrow only while the pointer is over the header row or the header has keyboard focus, so a dense header rests on its labels; the sorted column's arrow always shows, and on touch every arrow shows.
- A Table's stacked rows are compact blocks: the first cell titles the row, with the first labelled value, such as a status, beside it without its label, and an unlabelled cell, such as the row's menu of actions, on the same line; the other values follow two to a line, each under its label, in place of a line for each value. Put a value that reads on its own, such as a status, second. [Table](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/table.md#stacked-rows)
- A Table's stacked row groups its values more tightly: each label sits directly over its value, and the lines of values are a little further apart, so each pair reads as one and the row's details as one block.
- Tabs stand apart: a tab built from its `label` keeps a button's inline padding, so neighbouring tabs no longer overlap and their labels no longer run together, and the indicator is as wide as the label. A text button of your own as a tab takes no negative margin.
- A disabled tab takes the disabled text color, as a disabled button does, so it no longer looks like the tabs that are available.
- Under forced colors, the first tab of a horizontal Tabs list shows its focus ring again, in the system's color: lining its label up with the content had moved the ring onto a shape the system does not paint.
- Tabs show no fill or bar under the pointer: only a tab's label takes the text color, so the indicator alone marks the selected tab. Pressed, a tab also lays a faint state layer, a step past hover.
- The labels of the tabs not selected take a step stronger than the muted text, `--env-color-fg-muted-hover`, so a disabled tab's color stands clearly apart from them.
- A horizontal tab list lines its first label up with the content below it: the list reaches back by the first tab's inline padding, so the label no longer starts a step in from the panel's text. Each tab keeps its own box, so no two overlap.
- A Tabs rail with more tabs than fit starts with the chosen tab in view: the rail is its tabs' offset parent now, so it measures the chosen tab from its own start when it scrolls it into view. Inside a positioned container set in from the page's start, such as a centred frame, it once measured from the container's start and rested scrolled past the chosen tab.
- A vertical Tabs rail's tabs are as tall as a medium control, like the other navigation rows beside them, where the default large size made them a step taller; a small rail keeps its smaller tabs.
- A text button alone in a container as wide as its content, such as the name in an interactive list row, keeps its words in a line: its largest width allows for the margins that give its padding back, where its last word once wrapped onto a second line.
- Text no longer brings the dotted underline's style sheet, `env-hover-detail`, into a page that shows no HoverCard, Tooltip or RelativeTime, so a page of Cards, Lists or Text loads a little less CSS.
- A Textarea whose text runs past its `rows` clips that text at its padding, as a scroll box does at its edge, rather than showing the next line in the padding and under the bottom border; MarkdownEditor's text area does too.
- Textarea's resize edge shows its grip only under the pointer, while the field has focus and while it is dragged; at rest the edge is the frame alone.
- The rules of a text area's resize handle, `env-textarea-resize-handle`, are in a style sheet of their own that only Textarea, Composer and MarkdownEditor bring, so a page with TextField but no text area loads a little less CSS.
- Textarea's resize grip is two short diagonal hairlines in its end corner, inside the frame, rather than an accent line along the whole edge that read as a second underline, a bar in the middle that read as a sheet's handle or a short bar that read as a minus sign; it still shows only under the pointer, while the field has focus and while it is dragged. A fixed Textarea given `rows` is that many lines tall, also under 7.5 rem, and resizes down to them or to 7.5 rem, whichever is less; without `rows` it keeps the 7.5 rem start. MarkdownEditor's text area follows.
- `ThemePreferenceProvider` hydrates a page rendered on the server without a mismatch or a flash: on the server and as the page hydrates, it renders the default preference, writes nothing on the document root, and its boundary takes the look of the theme the [bootstrap script](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/setup.md) gave the root before the first paint; once hydrated, it renders the stored preference and the system's theme. Before, the server's boundary was light, so a saved or system dark theme showed light until the page had hydrated.
- A theme or contrast switch through ThemePreferenceProvider shows the new colors in one frame: transitions are suspended while the page restyles, rather than easing hundreds of colors over the next frames, and the document root takes the theme in the same frame as the provider's boundary. On a catalogue page, the switch paints in about 32 ms instead of 40 to 58, with about 38 ms of style work instead of 170. A provider with `applyToDocument={false}` leaves its transitions as they were.
- The time wheels of DatePicker and TimePicker take a calendar day's height, and the chosen hour and minute are round, as the chosen day is, rather than wide pills, so the wheels are narrower; a longer value, such as a day period, stays a pill. Beside the calendar, the Time heading shares the line of the month's name.
- In the wheels of TimePicker and of DatePicker with `granularity="minute"`, the chosen hour, minute and day period take the chosen calendar day's solid accent fill, `--env-color-bg-primary-emphasis`, rather than the selection's tint, and are pills, as the chosen day is round, so a chosen day and time look alike. A wheel with fewer values than it shows rows, such as minutes every 15, no longer loops, which showed a value at both its top and its bottom; its arrow keys still wrap around. A light fade at a wheel's edges hints that it scrolls, while every value it shows stays legible, and times outside `min` and `max` take the disabled color and are struck through rather than only faded, so they no longer look like the fade.
- TimePicker's and DatePicker's time wheels keep the fill on the row they turn to, rather than passing it over the rows on the way, and a row's text takes the fill's color only while it is on the fill, so a value the fill leaves is never white on the popup; the field shows the new time once the wheel comes to rest.
- The wheels of TimePicker, and of a DatePicker with a time, turn under a mouse dragging them, as they do under a finger: the rows follow the pointer while its button is down, and a quick drag let go glides on and eases into a row. A press that doesn't move still chooses the row under it.
- A danger Toast shows the error icon, as a danger Alert does, rather than the warning icon.
- Toast's notices glide from where they were drawn to their new place inside a transformed or zoomed container, such as a scaled preview of a screen: the stack measures layout pixels rather than the drawn boxes, so a notice no longer jumps part of the way, or past its place, as others arrive and leave.
- Toasts wait while a modal Dialog, Sheet, ConfirmDialog or CommandPalette is open in the same container: those showing step back, and new ones are held, until it closes, rather than covering the modal's actions, out of reach behind it, on a phone. Each then shows and is announced, for its full duration. See [Toast](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/toast.md).
- TableOfContents: the current label's color changes quickly, in `--env-motion-duration-fast` with `--env-motion-ease-enter`, ahead of the shape that marks the reader's place, so only one label reads as current; a label under the pointer takes its color quickly, from the first frame, and under a reduced-motion preference both change at once.
- Alert, Badge and Toast take the spacing and type of the approved Tonal design. An Alert's inset is 14 px above and below and 16 px at the sides, its title semibold in the body size 2 px above its message, which takes the small body size. A Badge has 3 px above and below its label and 10 px at the sides, edge included. A Toast has 16 px before its icon or message and 10 px between its parts.
- A disabled Toolbar.Root or Toolbar.Group disables its Toolbar.Link too: the link reports `aria-disabled`, has no destination, leaves the arrow keys' path and shows no tooltip, as a disabled button does.
- Tree keeps a row's description further from its label, three times the room between the row's other parts, so a muted description such as Shared never reads as part of the name.
- A Tree's rows under the pointer and pressed take quieter layers than a table's, three quarters of `--env-color-bg-row-hover` and the whole of it, so a passing pointer never outweighs the light tint of the selected row. See [Tree](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/components/tree.md).
- A page with a Checkbox or a labelled text field no longer has the browser work out the style of the whole page again whenever anything on it changes, such as a menu opening or a row being added: two of their style rules matched in a way that made every change anywhere count. Skeletons that mount together also line up their pulses once rather than each on its own, and skeleton text reads its roots before it sets them. Nothing looks different.
- The title and headline type styles are a clear step apart from body text: a title is 18 px and a headline 22 px in the comfortable scale (16 and 20 px compact), with tighter lines and letters drawn a touch closer, so a dialog's, a card's or a page's heading reads as one at a glance. `--env-type-title-letter-spacing` and `--env-type-headline-letter-spacing` carry the tracking, which envUI's own headings set.
- Text breaks inside a word only where the word is longer than its line: themes and components wrap with `overflow-wrap: break-word` in place of `anywhere`, so a table column, a cell or a button keeps room for its longest word, and short words such as a project's name no longer split at narrow widths. Code keeps breaking anywhere. A Link takes the wrapping of the text around it, so it stays on one line in a `nowrap` table cell.

## 1.0.0-prerelease.4 — 2026-10-25

The fourth prerelease of envUI 1.0. Its documentation is one file per page, which the catalogue shows to people, the Docs to developers and the package to coding agents, with each example's code once; one prompt and a hosted skill set envUI up with a coding agent in place of the package's own agent tooling; dev mode opens in a tab of its own, rebuilt on today's envUI; and Drawer gains nested sections and TableOfContents is new. It removes the `envui` command, its MCP server, the packaged skills and the agent plugin: the [migration guide](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md) says what replaces them.

### Major Changes

- One prompt sets envUI up with a coding agent, and the package's own agent tooling is gone. Agents read the documentation the package ships, which a few lines in the project's `AGENTS.md` lead them to.
  - The prompt `Set up envUI in my app: envui.envsemble.com/SKILL.md`, on the Docs' home with a copy button, leads an agent to the setup skill the site hosts. After a short checklist that waits for a yes, it installs envUI at an exact version from GitHub Packages with its styles and theme, adds the `AGENTS.md` pointer, and turns on the ESLint plugin and the Stylelint config at the same version; the pointer and the lint rules are recommended, and any step can be skipped. In an application with UI of its own, it changes none of it and offers to plan a migration. The site also serves `llms.txt`, an index of the Docs.
  - Removed: the `envui` command (`bin`) with `envui agents` and the `envui mcp` server; the envui-use, envui-migrate and envui-contribute skills under `skills/`; the `envui` agent plugin and its marketplaces; and `manifest.json`, `llms.txt` and `llms-full.txt` at the package root. The Docs' Agent skills page opens For agents.
  - Migration: remove an `envui` MCP server from the project's or client's configuration, copied skill folders, the plugin and its marketplace, and `npx --no envui agents --check` from CI; replace the index between `<!-- BEGIN envui -->` and `<!-- END envui -->` with the pointer; and read `docs/index.md`, `docs/generated/props.json` and `docs/generated/parts.json` where code read the manifest. Running the setup prompt does most of this. The [migration guide](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md) lists each step.

### Minor Changes

- Dev mode opens in a tab of its own, is rebuilt on today's envUI, and keeps up with the page.
  - The Dev mode button and Alt+Shift+D open the page in a new tab with dev mode over the whole window, marked by the `envui-dev` query parameter, which `installDevMode()` reads there; the page they came from stays as it was. Dev mode's tab has no close button and Esc doesn't leave it: closing the tab ends it. The handle's new `openInNewTab()` does the same from code; `open()`, `close()`, `toggle()` and the `open` option open and close dev mode over the page itself, as before, where Esc still closes it. `open()` called right after `installDevMode()` now always opens it.
  - The 3D view's toolbar is four dropdowns whose names show at once on hover: View, the angles laid out as they look at the page, Top above Front, Left and Right beside it and Tilted in the top right corner, each turning the view there with the whole page in view; Show, the planes' names, padding and gaps, only the selected and color by window class; Responsive, the responsive frame, then its device and orientation, which apply while it is on; and View settings, with a Performance panel of the frame rate and what measuring costs. How to move around is a button in the view's top right corner and dev mode's theme is at the foot of the node tree. Each control has one place: the Close button, Fit, the frame's × and its handles' double click are gone, and the Nodes and Inspector buttons leave the toolbar: each sidebar hides with the button in its header, which stays in that top corner of the window while the sidebar is hidden.
  - The sidebars resize by their inner edge, with the grip, keys and double click of a resizable Drawer, up to all the window leaves beside the other sidebar and a narrow view, and dev mode remembers their widths and which are hidden. The inspector's Details and Readout are Tabs, the readout's copy button is a CopyButton, and the readout's lines wrap to the sidebar, indented under their own start.
  - The responsive frame lays the page out wider or taller than the window and whatever holds the root, with whole planes: nothing outside the root, nor the window, cuts them off. While the page resizes, a plane whose picture is of another size shows its outline, name and spacing until the page settles; the pictures are then copied a few each frame. A transition or an animation that ends in the page, and a node that resizes, are measured again.
  - Dragging the 3D view always turns it: pressed on text selected across it, such as after Ctrl+A, the browser no longer drags the selection as a picture, which stopped the view turning. The wheel over the view scrolls the page and zooms smoothly rather than notch by notch, and hovering the node tree no longer renders the view.
  - Dev mode's style sheet is in envUI's components layer, its rules on its own parts of no specificity, from envUI's tokens; the view glides to an angle unless reduced motion is preferred, and the legend makes way in a narrow view.

- Drawer nests destinations in sections that open and close, such as a documentation site's pages: a destination's `children` show under it, indented, with a chevron that flips as Select's does, and the sections that hold the current destination open as it becomes current. A section with `href` or `onSelect` is a link or button with a button at its end, named with the new `messages.navigation.contents`, such as Projects contents; one with neither is only its heading, a button. Each section's button reports `aria-expanded`, and a closed section keeps its links in the page, hidden until found, so search engines follow them and find in page opens it; the expand animation stops under reduced motion. `expanded`, `defaultExpanded` and `onExpandedChange` keep the open sections, named by `href` or label. Rail and Bar show the top level, where a destination whose `children` hold the current one takes the current fill and reports `aria-current="true"`. The new parts are `env-drawer-branch`, `env-drawer-branch-row`, `env-drawer-toggle`, `env-drawer-chevron` and `env-drawer-children`.
- TableOfContents, the page's sections as links, nested by level, with the section the reader is in marked as they scroll: a short dash before each section, like a ruler, which swells in a smooth wave around the reader's place as the page scrolls: the current section's the longest and darkest, the next ones growing as the reader nears them, and the one under the pointer longer too; the sections still to come are quieter and nested sections step in, their dashes the same. It reads the headings in a container, `[2, 3]` by default through `levels`, giving those without an id one, or takes sections of your own as `items`, any element with an id under a label of yours. A link scrolls smoothly to its section, at once where reduced motion is preferred, below a sticky header's scroll margin, adds it to the address and moves focus to it. The landmark is named On this page, from `messages.tableOfContents`, and the current link takes `aria-current="location"`.
- `tooltipDelay` on Toolbar.Root sets how long, in milliseconds, the pointer rests on a Toolbar.Button or Toolbar.Link before its tooltip opens, 400 by default as Tooltip's `delay`; 0 shows every name at once, for a toolbar people scan by pointing, such as a canvas's view controls.
- CommandPalette shows an empty state in place of the list when there are no options: a quiet icon on a muted circle, a title and a quieter description, centred, which the polite status announces with the icon hidden from assistive technology. Before people type it invites a search, with the Search icon and Type to search (`messages.commandPalette.emptyQuery`); when the text finds nothing it shows SearchOff, No results (`messages.commandPalette.empty`) and `messages.commandPalette.emptyHint`, which quotes the text and suggests checking the spelling. `emptyLabel` still sets the title, and the new `emptyDescription` and `emptyIcon` set the description and the icon, `null` hiding either. The new `defaultItems` show while the search field is empty, in place of `items`, such as recent documents or suggestions under a group heading, and are highlighted and chosen like any result; the empty state shows only when there are none.

### Patch Changes

- Every component, pattern and foundation page of the documentation ships as one file per page, in one order: the frontmatter lists each example file once, with its role and caption, then Variants (Options on a foundation), Anatomy, Behaviour, Rules, Accessibility, Words to use and Compose, with each example's code once. The foundations gain the words, figures' legends and rules they had only in the catalogue.
- Slider's fill is empty at the minimum and fills the whole field at the maximum, in proportion between, and ends where the pointer chooses the value; near either end the handle keeps clear of the rounded edge. Dragging or double-clicking a slider no longer selects its label or value.
- Slider stretches a little when dragged past either end, as a rubber band: the value stays at the minimum or maximum while the field stretches toward the pointer from its other end and thins slightly, giving less the further the pointer goes, up to 6% of its length or half its height, and springs back once let go. The label and the value keep their shape and size. Only a drag stretches it, not a press or the keyboard, nor a disabled slider, and it doesn't stretch where reduced motion is preferred.
- Markdown keeps its elements when it renders again with a new `renderLink`, such as an inline function: the headings, links, code and tables it renders are the same elements, so a page's anchors, focus and scroll position survive, and only what changed renders again. TableOfContents follows the page's scrolling also after its sections render anew, where its marks once stayed on the section a link had chosen.
- DatePicker, TimePicker and MonthPicker open one picker card, with the same inset, corners, gaps and footer: TimePicker's popup takes DatePicker's spacing, so Now and Clear no longer sit at its edges, and the actions of all three line up with the body, the shortcut at the start and Clear at the end. The card's parts are `env-picker-popup`, `env-picker-popup-body`, `env-picker-popup-footer` and `env-picker-popup-footer-end`; the popups keep `env-date-picker-popup`, `env-time-picker-popup`, `env-month-picker-popup`, `env-date-picker-actions` and `env-date-picker-actions-end` beside them.

## 1.0.0-prerelease.3 — 2026-10-25

The third prerelease of envUI 1.0, and the first versioned since 1.0.0-prerelease.0: this section gathers the changes of prereleases 1 to 3. It brings Scaffold with the navigation's three forms, Bar, Rail and Drawer, Panes and TopBar; the layout primitives Row, Column, Grid and Box; skeletons from any surface; colors of its own for any element; ColorPicker on its own entry point; dev mode; and no drop shadows. It breaks code written for 1.0.0-prerelease.0: `@envsemble/envui-codemods` makes most of the changes, and the [migration guide](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md) covers the rest.

### Major Changes

- Any surface can become its own skeleton, and the Skeleton component goes.
  - `skeleton` on Surface, Card, List.Root and List.Item, Table.Root, Alert, Message, TopBar, Drawer, Rail, Bar, Dialog, Sheet, Popover, HoverCard, Menu.Root and Panes.Supporting shows the component, and everything inside it, as a skeleton: text becomes bars as long as the text, icons, avatars, media and controls become filled shapes of their size, a navigation's destinations bars and icon shapes, and backgrounds, borders and corners stay. Its root is marked `data-env-skeleton` and is the one busy region, `aria-busy`, that assistive technology hears; its content, in `env-skeleton-content`, a wrapper without a box of its own, is inert. A list, whose rows must be its children, is inert as a whole, with a visually hidden status, `env-skeleton-status`, beside it that says what is loading. Turning `skeleton` on or off renders the content anew. Every bar and shape pulses in step across the page, and keeps still with a reduced-motion preference.
  - `placeholders<Item>(count)` returns stand-in items, `Placeholder<Item>`: an `id`, `envui-placeholder-0` and so on, and every other field of `Item` possibly undefined, so a page writes `items ?? placeholders<Item>(3)` and renders its normal components while the items load. Text given as undefined, such as `title={item.title}`, is a bar of its text style's usual length; `isPlaceholder(item)` tells a stand-in apart by its id, also once serialized, and `useSkeleton()` tells a component of the application's own that it is inside a skeleton.
  - Skeleton and `SkeletonProps` are removed, with the `env-skeleton` class and its `data-emphasis`, and so is NavList.Skeleton, with `env-nav-list-skeleton`, `env-nav-list-skeleton-item` and its `data-icon-only`: render the components themselves with `skeleton`. Loading with no known shape is a Spinner with its label shown, `showLabel`. CodeDiff shows one while its highlighter loads.
  - Dev mode's `envui.layout.skeletonShift(root)` toggles a skeleton and reports, as `skeleton-shift` problems, the elements that move or resize when it loads, and media without an aspect ratio or a size of its own.
  - `v1-names` reports `Skeleton` and `SkeletonProps`, and `v1-classes` `env-skeleton`, `env-skeleton-highlight` and NavList.Skeleton's classes, with what to use instead; Skeleton's `tone` no longer moves to `emphasis`. `envui/no-renamed-exports` reports Skeleton too.

  Migration guide: [Skeletons](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Avatar hues are named for their colors: `blue` is `powder`, `teal` `seafoam`, `green` `sage`, `amber` `butter`, `red` `blush`, `violet` `periwinkle`, `purple` `orchid` and `slate` `oat`, in `hue`, `avatarHues`, `AvatarHue` and `avatarHue`, and as the values of `data-hue`: `[data-hue='blue']`, `[data-hue='teal']`, `[data-hue='green']`, `[data-hue='amber']`, `[data-hue='red']`, `[data-hue='violet']`, `[data-hue='purple']` and `[data-hue='slate']` are `[data-hue='powder']`, `[data-hue='seafoam']`, `[data-hue='sage']`, `[data-hue='butter']`, `[data-hue='blush']`, `[data-hue='periwinkle']`, `[data-hue='orchid']` and `[data-hue='oat']`. A name keeps the hue it had, now under its new name.

  The tokens follow: `--env-color-avatar-bg-teal` and the others are `--env-color-avatar-bg-seafoam` and so on, with `--env-color-avatar-fg-{hue}` and `--env-color-avatar-figure-{hue}` beside them, and each hue, powder included, has tokens of its own. `--env-color-avatar-bg`, `--env-color-avatar-fg` and `--env-color-avatar-figure` stay, the powder hue's.

  To migrate, `v1-props` rewrites a literal `hue`, such as `<Avatar hue="teal">` to `hue="seafoam"`, and `v1-classes` the token names; rename `data-hue` values in your own CSS by hand. A fill you set on an old `--env-color-avatar-bg-{hue}` for a color of your own now sits under the hue's deep foreground, so set `--env-color-avatar-fg-{hue}` and `--env-color-avatar-figure-{hue}` beside it, to a color that keeps 4.5:1 on that fill. The ESLint plugin and the Stylelint config know the new token names.

- CounterBadge folds into Badge: a Badge with `count` is a compact count.
  - `<Badge count={12} />` shows a count, a number or short text such as 99+, in a compact round pill with tabular figures, in place of a label. It takes the tones and variants of any badge: neutral and soft by default, with the figures in the default text color, and `tone="accent" variant="solid"` for the filled count that needs attention. Its `aria-label`, such as 12 unread, is read in place of the number.
  - CounterBadge and `CounterBadgeProps` are gone, and so are 0.x's Counter and `CounterProps`, which 1.0.0-prerelease.0 renamed to them: `<CounterBadge value tone>` is `<Badge count tone>`, with `variant="solid"` in the accent tone. Bar's counts and Toast's count of hidden notices are Badges with `count`.
  - The part classes `env-counter-badge` and `env-counter-badge-label` are `.env-badge[data-count]` and `env-badge-count-label`, and the hooks a control sets for the counts inside it, `--env-counter-badge-fg`, `-bg` and `-border`, are `--env-badge-count-fg`, `-bg` and `-border`.
  - `v1-structure` rewrites CounterBadge and Counter into a Badge with `count`, and reports a `tone` that is an expression; `v1-names` renames `CounterBadgeProps` and `CounterProps` to `BadgeProps`, and `v1-classes` renames the classes and hooks. `envui/no-renamed-exports` reports Counter with what replaces it.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- ColorPicker and ColorSwatch move to their own entry point, `@envsemble/envui/color`, rebuilt on React Aria's color hooks (`@react-aria/color`, `@react-stately/color` and `@react-aria/i18n`), which the application installs, as the setup guide lists; an application that never imports the entry never loads them. The main entry exports neither any more.
  - **ColorPicker** is compact: a saturation and brightness area, a hue slider, an alpha slider and field with `alpha`, a format menu and quiet fields for hex, RGB and HSL, each channel named by its letter beside its number, such as R, G and B, a copy button, the browser's eyedropper where it has one (`eyeDropper`), and preset `swatches`. `formats` chooses the formats and their order, and `copy` the copy button. `value` and `defaultValue` take a CSS color string, hex, `rgb()` or `hsl()`, or React Aria's `Color`; `onValueChange(value, color)` receives the string in `valueFormat`, `#rrggbb` by default, and the `Color`; `name` submits the string through a hidden input. `showLabel`, `description`, `error`, `disabled` and `readOnly` stay; `variant` is gone, and `ref` reaches the picker's root rather than the HEX field. Fields commit on Enter and as focus leaves, and the arrow keys step them.
  - **ColorPickerButton** is a compact button that shows a color as a swatch and its value, named after its label, and opens a ColorPicker in a popover.
