  - **ColorSwatch** is a small rounded square with a checkerboard under a translucent color. `format` writes its value beside it, `label` a name, and `size="sm"` makes it smaller; alone, it is an image named by its color's description, such as dark purple. As the icon of an IconButton in its default variant, the button is a rounded square with no fill at rest, which shows on hover, focus and press.
  - EnvUIProvider's `messages.colorPicker` holds `format`, `formatButton`, `hex`, `rgb`, `hsl`, `hexField`, the channel letters `redShort` to `alphaShort`, `copy`, `eyeDropper`, `swatches` and `button`; `description`, `invalid`, `hexLabel`, `restored`, `hue`, `area` and `areaValue` are gone, as React Aria names the channels and the area in the provider's locale.
  - Parts: `env-color-picker-plane`, `env-color-picker-saturation`, `env-color-picker-saturation-slider`, `env-color-picker-saturation-thumb`, `env-color-picker-hue-control`, `env-color-picker-hue-thumb`, `env-color-picker-hue-track` and `env-color-picker-status` are gone. The area is `env-color-picker-area`, its thumb and the sliders' `env-color-picker-thumb`, the sliders `env-color-picker-slider` (`env-color-picker-hue`, `env-color-picker-alpha`) with `env-color-picker-track`, and the fields `env-color-picker-fields`; a swatch's color is `env-color-swatch-sample`.

  The `v1-props` and `v1-entries` codemods move the props and the imports.

  Migration guide: [Color picker and swatches](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- ColorPicker's `formats` with two or more formats shows them at once, instead of limiting the format menu to them as in 1.0.0-prerelease.2: `formats={['hex', 'rgb', 'hsl']}` shows each format's fields on a row of its own, in that order, with a copy button of its own, all editing the same color, their letters and numbers lined up from row to row. Each row is a group named after its format, and its copy button takes the new `messages.colorPicker.copyFormat`, such as Copy color as RGB. With one format, or without `formats`, the picker shows one format at a time as before, and the format menu stays the default. ColorPickerButton passes `formats` to its picker too.

  To migrate, check each ColorPicker and ColorPickerButton with two or more `formats`: keep them to show those formats together, or remove `formats` to keep one format at a time with the menu, which then offers hex, RGB and HSL. A menu of only some formats is gone; to offer one format, pass it alone, such as `formats={['hex']}`.

- Any single element can take colors of its own, Material's way. envUI's own colors don't change.
  - **Component color properties.** Each component has public color properties on its root, named `--env-<component>-<role>`, after Material's component tokens: container, content, outline and icon where they apply, selected and selected-content for what is chosen, current or on, and indicator for a field's focused edge and a slider's fill, such as `--env-button-container`, `--env-list-item-selected` or `--env-text-field-indicator`. Set them on one instance through `style` or a selector that matches it: they are registered so they don't inherit, so one set on a wrapper colors nothing inside it. Button, IconButton and a toolbar's toggles, Card, Surface, Badge, Alert, Avatar, Tooltip, Toast, List rows, Menu items, the items of Bar, Rail and Drawer, Tabs, SegmentedControl, TextField, Textarea, Select, Checkbox, Radio, Switch, Slider and Combobox's chips have them. They are public and versioned: `docs/generated/parts.json` lists them with the parts, with what each colors, the manifest and the MCP server's `get_component` return them, and each component's page names them under Anatomy.
  - **States follow the colors you set.** With no color property set, every component paints its designed colors in every state, as before. Once its container or content is set, hover, keyboard focus and press lay the content color over the container at the new tokens `--env-state-hover-opacity`, `--env-state-focus-opacity` and `--env-state-pressed-opacity`, so it keeps working states.
  - **A nested Theme still recolors an area:** override the semantic `--env-color-*` tokens on it, with their hover and pressed values. [Customize and extend](docs/customizing.md) describes both ways.
  - **Renamed component tokens.** `--env-list-item-fill` is `--env-list-item-container`; `--env-alert-tint`, `--env-alert-edge` and `--env-alert-tone` are `--env-alert-container`, `--env-alert-outline` and `--env-alert-icon`; `--env-toast-fill` and `--env-toast-edge` are `--env-toast-container` and `--env-toast-outline`. The old tokens inherited and the color properties don't: a token set on a wrapper reached the component inside it, and its color property set there colors nothing, so set it on the component itself. `envui-codemods v1-classes` renames them, also where a renamed class gives one of the old names, and reports each declaration in a rule whose selector doesn't end at the component, such as `.my-alert` rather than `.my-alert .env-alert`; a style object or `setProperty` call on a wrapper element it can't tell, and the migration guide says so.
  - **Removed component tokens.** A toast's hover and pressed tokens, `--env-toast-fill-hover` and `--env-toast-fill-pressed`, are gone: set `--env-toast-container`, and the dismiss button's hover and press become layers of the toast's text color. `v1-classes` reports them.
  - **`--env-badge-outline` changed meaning.** In prerelease.1 it was a private border shorthand, such as `0.0625rem solid <color>`; now it is the badge's public outline color. Write `--env-badge-outline: <color>`: a shorthand is an invalid color, and the badge draws no edge.

  Migration guide: [Color properties and state layers](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- envUI's components lay out their own parts with Row, Column and Grid instead of flex and grid rules of their own, so the parts that arrange others carry `data-env-layout` (and dev mode names them). Their part classes, state attributes and look are unchanged: what was a grid is a Grid, a flex row or column a Row or Column, and a part's own class still overrides the primitive's rules.

  Row and Column zero the block margins of their direct children (and Row the inline ones too), so content of yours placed directly in such a part loses its default margins, such as a `<p>` passed to List.Item, Message's actions, Alert's `action`, Card's header or Tabs. Give it a class with the margin you want, or wrap it. Parts that were grids, such as Message, Popover and Field, keep their children's margins. Migration guide: [Layout primitives](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).
  - Feedback and floating surfaces: Alert, Dialog's header, Meter, Progress, Popover, HoverCard, CommandPalette, the popups of Menu, Select, Combobox, DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker.
  - Content: Card's header, CodeBlock's heading, CalendarHeatmap, List, Message and MessageList.
  - Form controls: Field and its error, Calendar, the month grid, the panes, time column and actions of DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker, DropZone, Composer, MarkdownEditor's write pane, RadioGroup and Slider's heading.
  - Navigation and actions: Breadcrumbs, DocumentTabs' bar and strip, Menubar, Pager's row, Pagination, Tabs, Toolbar and its groups, and PanelDock's dock, panels, tab strip and header parts. A Toolbar or Toolbar.Group given `render`, such as a ButtonGroup, is now laid out by that element's own rules, so a ButtonGroup keeps its connected spacing.

- Drawer, the panel that slides in over the page, is Sheet, after Material's side and bottom sheets; the name Drawer is the navigation drawer's. Sheet takes Drawer's props, and `side="bottom"` for a bottom sheet, as wide as the window up to 40 rem and as tall as its content, with rounded top corners and a swipe down to close.
  - Its widths follow the window classes of the `env-window` container instead of the viewport: a `md` side sheet takes the whole width of a compact window, below 37.5 rem, and a `lg` one of a compact or medium window, below 52.5 rem.
  - The part classes `env-drawer-popup`, `env-drawer-viewport` and `env-drawer-backdrop` are `env-sheet-popup`, `env-sheet-viewport` and `env-sheet-backdrop`, with the same state attributes, `data-side`, `data-width`, `data-starting-style`, `data-ending-style` and `data-swiping`.
  - `v1-structure` turns a 0.x `<Dialog placement="right">` into a Sheet. 0.x had no Drawer, and the name is the navigation drawer's now, so no codemod renames one: rename `Drawer` and `DrawerProps` from the prerelease to `Sheet` and `SheetProps`, and the `env-drawer-*` classes to `env-sheet-*`, by hand. `v1-structure` reports a Drawer without `destinations`, and `v1-classes` the classes.

  Migration guide: [Overlays and feedback](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- EmptyState is a pattern now, not a component: it carries no behavior, and its parts are envUI's own.
  - EmptyState and `EmptyStateProps` are gone. The empty states pattern, `docs/patterns/empty-states.md`, builds one from a Column with an icon, a Text heading, a Text sentence and a Button: at the start of a card or under a list's toolbar, or centred with a larger icon where it takes a region's place. Its notes keep what EmptyState took care of: the heading's level in the page's outline, which `headingLevel` set, and the alert role of a failure, which `tone="danger"` set.
  - The part classes `env-empty-state` and `env-empty-state-description` go, with `data-tone` and `data-align`. CodeDiff shows a failure to load with its own parts, `env-code-diff-failed` and `env-code-diff-failed-icon`, as an alert. The `empty-state` page opens the pattern.
  - `v1-names` reports EmptyState where it is used, and `v1-classes` its classes and 0.x's `env-empty-copy`; no codemod rewrites them. `envui/no-renamed-exports` reports them with the pattern to use instead.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Card's feature layout is built from envUI's components now: Card keeps its surface and its parts, and a feature card is a Card holding envUI's own components.
  - Card's `layout` and `icon` props are gone, with the `data-layout` attribute, `[data-layout='feature']`, `[data-layout='stack']` and the part classes `env-card-layout` and `env-card-icon`. A card with actions is `.env-card[data-actions]`, and a card's title is an `h3` by default whatever it holds.
  - The feature card on Card's page, `docs/components/card.md`, builds one from a Card holding a Row with the icon in muted Text, a Column with a headline Text heading and a Text sentence, and the Button that starts the feature, which moves below the text in a narrow card. The page keeps the heading's level in the page's outline.
  - `v1-props` drops `layout="stack"` and reports `layout="feature"` and `icon`, `v1-structure` reports 0.x's FeatureCard instead of writing the feature layout, and `v1-classes` reports `env-feature-card-*`, `env-card-layout`, `env-card-icon` and `[data-layout]` on a card.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Popover and HoverCard size to their content, from 12 rem up to their `width`, so a short note or a single field no longer sits in a wide empty card. Menus, tooltips, popovers, hover cards and the date, month and time pickers' popups are never wider than the room their positioner finds beside the trigger (`--available-width`), rather than the window less 2 rem, so they stay on screen in any frame.

  Migration: `width` (`md` 20 rem, `lg` 28 rem) is now the maximum, not a fixed width. To keep a fixed width, set `inline-size` through `className`, such as `.rename-popover { inline-size: 28rem; }`.

  Migration guide: [Overlays and feedback](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- IconButton is the one icon-only button, and the one close button.
  - CloseButton and `CloseButtonProps` are gone: a close, dismiss or remove button is an IconButton with `variant="ghost"`, `icon={<Close />}` and an `aria-label` in the application's language, as the Button page's close and remove section shows. Dialog, Sheet, Panes, Alert, Toast, Combobox, DocumentTabs and PanelDock render theirs that way. The small ones, Alert's and Toast's dismiss, Combobox's chip remove, and DocumentTabs' and PanelDock's tab close, keep CloseButton's touch-size hit area under a coarse pointer through IconButton's new hook `--env-icon-button-hit-area`, which they set to the touch size; Dialog's, Sheet's and Panes' close buttons are full size and need none. An application's own small close button sets the hook to keep the hit area. The part class `env-close-button` goes, and so does `messages.common.close`, which only CloseButton read.
  - `Button iconOnly` is gone: `<IconButton icon aria-label>` replaces it, and takes `href` for an icon-only link.
  - `v1-structure` rewrites `<CloseButton>` into the IconButton, importing `Close` from `@envsemble/envui/icons/Close`, and writes `aria-label="Close"` where it had no name, which it reports to translate. It rewrites `<Button iconOnly>` into `<IconButton>`, reporting those without a `variant`, which were primary where IconButton is secondary, and those that set `fullWidth`, `underline` or a `tone` without a `variant`, which IconButton doesn't take; a structure case's `reportProps` reports such props. `v1-classes` reports `env-close-button`. A structure case can now add a component imported from another module, such as an icon.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Stat is a pattern now, not a component: a figure carries no behavior of its own, and applications arrange their figures differently.
  - Stat, with Stat.Root, Stat.Group and the `Stat.RootProps`, `Stat.GroupProps`, `Stat.Trend` and `Stat.TrendTone` types, is gone. The key figures pattern, `docs/patterns/key-figures.md`, builds a figure from a muted Text label, a headline Text figure in tabular digits and a Badge that says the trend in words, in its tone and with a trending icon, and a group from a Surface with `role="group"` around a Grid of even columns. Its notes keep what Stat took care of: the trend said in words, tabular digits, and the group's name.
  - The part classes `env-stat`, `env-stat-label`, `env-stat-value`, `env-stat-footer`, `env-stat-trend`, `env-stat-description` and `env-stat-group` go, with the `data-direction` and `data-tone` of the trend. The `stat` page opens the pattern.
  - `v1-names` reports Stat, 0.x's Stat and StatGroup and their types where they are used, and `v1-classes` reports the `env-stat-*` classes; no codemod rewrites them. `envui/no-renamed-exports` reports them with the pattern to use instead.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Row, Column, Grid and Box, the layout primitives, replace Stack: thin wrappers over flex and grid that size to their content and never paint.
  - `<Stack direction="row">` is `<Row>`, with `wrap`, and any other Stack a `<Column>`; `gap`, `padding`, `align` and `justify` take Stack's values, the gap and the padding as steps of the spacing scale. Row and Column have no gap of their own, where Stack's was 1 rem.
  - Grid lays content out in columns: `columns={3}` for three equal columns, a template such as `columns="minmax(0, 2fr) minmax(0, 1fr)"`, or `columns={{ min: '14rem' }}` for as many as fit. Box is a plain block for `padding`, a named size container (`container`) or a named region.
  - Each renders `data-env-layout` (`box`, `row`, `column` or `grid`), `name` as `data-env-name`, and its options as attributes such as `data-gap`, and takes `render`, `className` and `ref`. Its rules have no specificity, so any class overrides them; the `env-stack` class and `StackProps` are gone.
  - The `v1-stack` codemod rewrites each Stack, after `v1-structure` and `v1-render`, and reports a `direction` that is an expression. `envui/no-renamed-exports` no longer reports `Row` and `RowProps`, 0.x's list row, as 1.0 exports `Row` again.

  Migration guide: [Layout primitives](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- envUI draws no drop shadows. Menus, Select and Combobox lists, popovers, hover cards, pickers, rich text suggestions, docked panels, tooltips, toasts, dialogs, sheets and the command palette separate by their fill, their edge and, for modals, the scrim.
  - The elevation edge, `--env-elevation-{1|2|3}-border`, is clearly visible now, one color for every level: about 2:1 against a popup and the page, and at least 1.6:1 against every surface a popup floats over. In high contrast it stays the 4.5:1 boundary. In dark, the edge of tooltips and toasts, `--env-color-border-{neutral|success|info|danger}-strong`, is this edge too.
  - The elevation shadows `--env-elevation-{0|1|2|3}-shadow` are removed, and with them the 0.7 names `--elevation-{0|1|2|3}-shadow`, which no longer take the prefix. `v1-classes` reports each use. Delete the `box-shadow`. If your own floating layer relied on it, give the layer its level's edge instead: `border: 1px solid var(--env-elevation-2-border)`.
  - A row that moves in a reorderable list takes the elevation edge as a ring, and the color picker's thumbs take a ring outside their white edge. Neither has a shadow any more. Docked panels and the active one are no longer set apart by shadows: the active docked panel's edge takes the accent, `--env-color-border-accent-emphasis`, in every theme and contrast.
  - Focus rings, halos, inset edges and selection bars drawn with `box-shadow` are rings, not shadows, and stay.

  Migration guide: [Tokens](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- The underline field is gone: TextField, SearchField, Textarea, MarkdownEditor and RichTextEditor take `variant` `outline`, `floating` (TextField and SearchField) or `ghost`, and no longer `underline`, with its state value `data-variant="underline"` on `env-text-field-control`. The `v1-props` codemod drops `variant="underline"`, and 0.x's `variant="plain"`, so the field takes the default outline, and reports each.

  Migration guide: [Fields and choice controls](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- OrganizationSwitcher is a pattern now, not a component: it is a Menu that lists the organizations, which applications arrange as they need.
  - OrganizationSwitcher, `OrganizationSwitcherProps`, `OrganizationSwitcherItem` and `messages.organizationSwitcher` are gone. The organization switcher pattern, `docs/patterns/organization-switcher.md`, builds it from a Menu.Root whose trigger is a secondary Button that names the active organization, a Menu.RadioGroup of Menu.RadioItems with the person's role as each `description`, and a Menu.Item that creates one; before the first organization, the create action is a Button of its own. The Menu page links to it.
  - The part classes `env-organization-switcher`, `env-organization-switcher-trigger`, `env-organization-switcher-trigger-label`, `env-organization-switcher-header`, `env-organization-switcher-heading`, `env-organization-switcher-options`, `env-organization-switcher-option`, `env-organization-switcher-description` and `env-organization-switcher-create` go, with the `data-checked` and `data-highlighted` they carried: the pattern's rows are Menu's own `env-menu-item`, which carry them.
  - `v1-names` reports OrganizationSwitcher, and 0.x's OrganizationMenu and their types, where they are used, and `v1-classes` the `env-organization-switcher-*` and `env-organization-menu-*` classes; no codemod rewrites them, and `v1-props` no longer renames OrganizationSwitcher's props. `envui/no-renamed-exports` reports OrganizationMenu with the pattern to use instead.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Page is invisible structure: `data-env-layout="page"`, `name` as `data-env-name`, and the `env-page` container for its content. Its gutter follows the window class of the region it sits in, 1 rem in a compact one and 1.5 rem from medium, where it grew with the viewport from 1 to 2 rem.

  PageHeader is built from Row and Column. A sticky one sticks at the top of the area that scrolls, under a Scaffold's top bar where the document scrolls (`--env-scaffold-sticky-top`), and no longer reads `--env-frame-sticky-top`. What scrolls into view in its Page's content keeps clear of it by a scroll margin: it still publishes its height as `--env-page-header-block-size` on its Page, and until script runs CSS reckons the height from its rows; put content that should stay in view under it in its `toolbar`.

  Migration guide: [The app frame](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Part names are public; component tokens are private.
  - **Part classes and state attributes are public and versioned.** Every part of a component has a class, `env-<component>-<part>`, such as `.env-card-header`, and a component shows its state in `data-*` attributes, such as `[data-tone='danger']`. An application's CSS may target them, and from now on a release that renames or removes one is a major release with a migration note. `docs/generated/parts.json` lists every component's root class, its parts with what each one is, and its state attributes with their values; the manifest gives each component page its `parts`, the MCP server's `get_component` returns them, and llms.txt names each component's root classes.
  - **Component tokens are private.** The custom properties a component declares for itself, such as `--env-card-padding` or `--env-badge-fill`, may change in any release. Set the system tokens, such as `--env-space-4` or `--env-color-bg-muted`, instead.
  - **The `no-private-classes` rules are removed** from the ESLint plugin (`envui/no-private-classes`) and the Stylelint config (`envui/no-private-classes`), since the classes they reported are public now. To migrate, delete the rule from any configuration or disable comment that names it: ESLint and Stylelint report a rule they don't know.

  Migration guide: [Part names and component tokens](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- AppShell, its parts, Sidebar and SidebarTrigger are gone, with `useAppShell`, `useSidebar`, `readAppShellCookie` and their types (`AppShellState`, `PerSide`, `PersistOptions`, `SidebarSide`, `SidebarSnapshot`, `FrameStateProps`, `SidebarChangeReason`, `SidebarTriggerProps`). An app frame is an arrangement every application makes its own: build it on Scaffold, with a TopBar in its `topBar` and the navigation forms in `navigation={{ bar, rail, drawer }}`, which it shows by its own width, as the app frame pattern shows. The body scrolls rather than the document, and nothing collapses, so there is no state to keep.
  - The part classes go: `env-app-shell`, `env-app-shell-header`, `env-app-shell-header-start`, `env-app-shell-header-title`, `env-app-shell-header-end`, `env-app-shell-main`, `env-app-shell-loading-bar`, `env-app-shell-skip-links` and `env-app-shell-skip-link`, which are `env-scaffold`, `env-top-bar`, `env-top-bar-start`, `env-top-bar-title`, `env-top-bar-actions`, `env-scaffold-body`, `env-top-bar-loading-bar`, `env-scaffold-skip-links` and `env-scaffold-skip-link`; and `env-sidebar`, `env-sidebar-header`, `env-sidebar-content`, `env-sidebar-footer`, `env-sidebar-group`, `env-sidebar-group-heading`, `env-sidebar-group-label`, `env-sidebar-group-action`, `env-sidebar-handle`, `env-sidebar-panel`, `env-sidebar-trigger`, `env-sidebar-trigger-glyphs`, `env-sidebar-trigger-icon`, `env-sidebar-trigger-mark` and `env-sidebar-trigger-tooltip`, whose place the navigation forms and the Scaffold's slots `env-scaffold-bar`, `env-scaffold-rail` and `env-scaffold-drawer` take.
  - Their state attributes go: `data-breakpoint`, `data-dividers`, `data-end`, `data-header-span` (Scaffold's `data-top-bar-span`), `data-loading`, `data-mobile`, `data-mobile-only`, `data-ready`, `data-scroll`, `data-title-bar` (TopBar's), `data-align`, `data-collapsible`, `data-dock-at`, `data-dragging`, `data-fill`, `data-frame`, `data-held`, `data-peek`, `data-popup-held`, `data-rail`, `data-region`, `data-resizable`, `data-resizing`, `data-side`, `data-state`, `data-width`, `data-keep-shown`, `data-mark` and `data-sidebar-trigger`. Scaffold's `data-compact`, `data-medium`, `data-expanded` and `data-large` name the form each window class shows.
  - The token `--env-app-shell-header-height` is `--env-top-bar-height`, which `v1-classes` rewrites; `--env-frame-sticky-top` and `--env-frame-header-block-size` go, as sticky content sticks at the top of the Scaffold's body.
  - SearchTrigger no longer shows only its icon by itself in a sidebar's rail: set `iconOnly`.
  - The `v1-app-shell` codemod goes: no codemod rewrites an app frame. `v1-names` reports AppShell, AppShellSkeleton, Sidebar, SidebarTrigger, the hooks and the types, `AppShellContextValue` and `SidebarApi` among them, wherever they are used, and the migration guide shows a frame before and after. `v1-classes` renames the part classes that carry on, `env-app-shell-header` to `env-top-bar` and the others as listed above, and reports `env-app-shell` itself and the `env-sidebar-*` classes.

  Migration guide: [The app frame](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- NavList and NavList.Skeleton are gone: Bar, Rail and Drawer show an application's destinations from one array.
  - A NavList.Item is a destination in the array: `selected` is `current`, a count in `end` is `count`, NavList.Group's `label` is each destination's `group`, and `iconOnly` in a sidebar's rail is the Rail. End content other than a count has no place in a destination.
  - The `navList` messages are `navigation`: `itemCount` keeps its meaning, and `itemEnd` is gone.
  - A Tabs item's button is a Button or an IconButton: a NavList.Item as a tab is a ghost Button with `startIcon`.
  - NavList's parts are gone: `env-nav-list`, `env-nav-list-group`, `env-nav-list-group-label`, `env-nav-list-group-list`, `env-nav-list-item`, `env-nav-list-item-dot`, `env-nav-list-item-end`, `env-nav-list-skeleton` and `env-nav-list-skeleton-item`, with their states `data-selected`, `data-icon-only` and `data-rail`. The navigation forms' parts are `env-bar-*`, `env-rail-*` and `env-drawer-*`, the current item with `data-current`.
  - No codemod rewrites a NavList, since its items are elements and a destination is data. `v1-names` reports NavList, and with `envui/no-renamed-exports` 0.x's NavigationItem and NavigationItemProps, with what to use instead, and `v1-classes` reports the `env-navigation-*` and `env-nav-list-*` classes instead of renaming them.

  Migration guide: [Navigation forms](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- SplitPane and ListDetail are gone: Panes replaces them, one scaffold for two panes side by side with two behaviors in less room, `supporting` and `list-detail`, which CSS decides from its own width instead of measuring it.
  - `<ListDetail.Root detailShown onBack backLabel>` is `<Panes.Root behavior="list-detail" open onOpenChange backLabel>`, with Panes.List and Panes.Detail; the `v1-structure` codemod rewrites it, and reports its sizes, its `stackBelow`, which is `splitAt` at a window class, and its `resizeLabel`, as the divider is Panes.Divider, a part of its own. SplitPane is rewritten by hand, as supporting Panes with Panes.Main, an optional Panes.Divider and Panes.Supporting.
  - Sizes are in rem: `size`, `defaultSize`, `minSize` and `maxSize` are the resized pane's width, and `persist` keeps it.
  - The part classes go: `env-split-pane`, `env-split-pane-pane`, `env-split-pane-handle`, `env-split-pane-probe`, `env-list-detail`, `env-list-detail-list`, `env-list-detail-detail` and `env-list-detail-back`, which are `env-panes`, `env-panes-main`, `env-panes-supporting`, `env-panes-list`, `env-panes-detail`, `env-panes-divider` and `env-panes-back`. Their state attributes go: `data-orientation`, `data-axis` (the divider keeps it, as every resize handle does), `data-stack-below`, `data-stacked` and `data-detail-shown`; Panes has `data-behavior`, `data-split-at` and `data-open`.
  - A vertical split pane has no counterpart.
  - `v1-classes` renames the classes of SplitPane and ListDetail to Panes', `env-split-pane-handle` to `env-panes-divider` and `env-list-detail-back` to `env-panes-back`, and reports `env-split-pane-probe` and the states Panes no longer sets, such as `[data-stacked]`.

  Migration guide: [Split panes](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- The templates are gone from the documentation: the app frame, index, details, settings, dashboard, sign-in and error pages, their screens in `docs/examples/`, and the manifest's `templates`. The MCP server's `list_templates` and `get_template` tools go with them; `plan` points to the patterns, and `list_patterns`, `get_pattern` and `get_example` serve their examples. The app frame is a pattern now, `docs/patterns/app-frame.md`: a Scaffold with a TopBar and the same destinations as a Bar, a Rail or a Drawer.

  Migration guide: [Templates and agent tools](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- SearchTrigger is gone: CommandPalette's page builds the button that opens the palette from a secondary Button with the Search icon, its label and the shortcut's keys, or from a ghost IconButton in a Tooltip, and the application listens for ⌘K or Ctrl+K itself, once.
  - SearchTrigger and `SearchTriggerProps` go, with `messages.searchTrigger`, the part classes `env-search-trigger`, `env-search-trigger-label`, `env-search-trigger-shortcut` and `env-search-trigger-tooltip`, and the page `components/search-trigger`, which opens CommandPalette's page now.
  - Button takes `end`: quiet content after the label, such as the Kbd of a shortcut, in the button's content color with outlined key caps, so it keeps its contrast on every variant, and at the far end of a button wider than its content, the part `env-button-end`. Kbd takes `--env-kbd-separator-fg` for the + between keys.
  - `v1-names` reports SearchTrigger and `SearchTriggerProps` where they are used, and `v1-classes` the `env-search-trigger-*` classes, each with a pointer to CommandPalette's page; no codemod rewrites them.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- LoadingState folds into Spinner: a Spinner with `showLabel` shows its label beside it.
  - `<Spinner label="Loading project" showLabel />` is a status line for loading with no known shape: a busy `role="status"` that centres the spinner and its muted label in its row, the label read as the status. Without a `label` it says Loading, from EnvUIProvider's `messages.common.loading`. The label is `env-spinner-label`, and the spinner carries `data-show-label`. Without `showLabel`, a Spinner is as it was: a progress bar named by `label`, or hidden.
  - LoadingState and `LoadingStateProps` are gone: `<LoadingState label>` is `<Spinner label showLabel>`, and `env-loading-state` and `env-loading-state-label` are `.env-spinner[data-show-label]` and `env-spinner-label`. CodeDiff shows its loading so.
  - `v1-structure` rewrites a LoadingState without children into the Spinner and reports the children of one that has them; `v1-names` renames `LoadingStateProps` to `SpinnerProps`, and `v1-classes` renames the classes. A structure case can be `childless`, for elements without children. `envui/no-renamed-exports` reports LoadingState with what replaces it.

  Migration guide: [Folded components and patterns](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- A Table with `narrowLayout="stack"` stacks its rows through a container query on its region instead of measuring it in script, so server-rendered markup and the first paint already stack in a narrow region and nothing moves as the page hydrates. `stackBelow` still takes any CSS length or a number of pixels.
  - The `env-table-stack-probe` part, the invisible element the table measured, is gone. Migration: nothing to change unless your styles targeted it, which `v1-classes` reports.
  - A stacking region's font size is now `stackBelow / 37.5`, so that `37.5em` in the container query is `stackBelow`; the table and its cells set their own text, so only text placed directly in the region, outside the table, would follow it.
  - A stacking region is now the `env-table` size container and fills the width its context gives it (`inline-size: 100%`), as a container must; give a stacking table a width where it sits in a row that sizes to its content.
  - `data-stacked` and `onStackedChange` still report the layout once the table is in the document. Style stacked rows under `@container env-table (width < 37.5em)`, where the region makes 37.5em its `stackBelow`, rather than through `data-stacked`, so they apply before hydration.

  Migration guide: [Navigation and collections](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- envUI responds to containers at window classes rather than to the viewport: compact below 37.5 rem, medium from 37.5, expanded from 52.5 and large from 75 rem, which `windowClasses`, `WindowClass` and the `--env-window-class-*` tokens hold.
  - The outermost theme boundary, the themed document root or the Theme around the application, is the `env-window` size container, and takes the whole width its parent gives it. Style sheets write compact first and switch up, as in `@container env-window (width >= 37.5rem)`, so content without a window stays compact. A region becomes a window of its own with `<Box container="env-window">`.
  - Where the document root isn't themed, a portal's boundary is the window: a popup's is a box out of the page's flow, as wide as the document, and a modal surface's (Dialog, ConfirmDialog, Sheet, CommandPalette) has no box: the surface's positioner, fixed over the viewport, is the window instead, so the scrim and the surface keep their own layers under the menus, toasts and tooltips opened over them. Under a themed root, popups' boundaries have no box, as before.
  - Card's feature layout and PageHeader take their wide layout from 37.5 rem of their own width (38 and 32 rem before), and DatePicker sets its time beside the calendar from a medium `env-window` rather than a viewport of 32 rem.
  - The viewport breakpoint helpers and tokens of earlier prereleases (`breakpoints`, `Breakpoint`, `breakpointQuery`, `useBreakpoint`, `useMediaQuery`, `MediaQueryOptions` and `--env-breakpoint-*`) are gone: `v1-names` reports the exports and `v1-classes` the tokens.

  Migration guide: [Window classes and container queries](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- WindowFrame and AccountMenu are patterns now, not components: they are arrangements of other components that applications arrange differently.
  - WindowFrame, with WindowFrame.Root, WindowFrame.TitleBar and WindowFrame.Content, is a Scaffold with `topBarSpan="full"` and a `<TopBar titleBar>`, whose body scrolls below the title bar; its `bodyRef` is the content's ref. The window frame pattern shows it. The part classes `env-window-frame`, `env-window-frame-content`, `env-window-frame-title-bar`, `env-window-frame-title-bar-layout`, `env-window-frame-title-bar-start`, `env-window-frame-title-bar-center` and `env-window-frame-title-bar-end` go: a title bar is `.env-top-bar[data-title-bar]`, with `env-top-bar-start`, `env-top-bar-title` and `env-top-bar-actions`.
  - AccountMenu, with AccountMenu.ThemeSubmenu and AccountMenu.OrganizationSubmenu, is a Menu.Root whose trigger is an AvatarButton, with a Menu.Header of the Avatar and the name, and a Menu.Submenu around a ThemeRadioGroup for the theme. The account menu pattern shows it. The part classes `env-account-menu`, `env-account-menu-header`, `env-account-menu-header-text`, `env-account-menu-header-name`, `env-account-menu-header-description`, `env-account-menu-trigger`, `env-account-menu-trigger-text`, `env-account-menu-trigger-name` and `env-account-menu-trigger-description` go, and with the trigger its `data-popup-open`, which every Menu.Root trigger still has.
  - The `v1-structure` codemod no longer rewrites 0.x's WindowFrame, or SplitView, into 1.0.0-prerelease.0's parts: `v1-names` reports WindowFrame, TitleBar, SplitView and AccountMenu where they are used. `v1-classes` renames WindowFrame's parts to the TopBar's and the Scaffold's, as listed above, and reports `env-window-frame` itself, `env-window-frame-title-bar-layout` and the `env-account-menu-*` classes.

  Migration guide: [Shell, dock, editors and code](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

### Minor Changes

- Accordion, a list of sections on Base UI's Accordion: `Accordion.Root` holds `Accordion.Item`s, each a heading that shows and hides its panel, separated by hairlines across the full width, a line under every item. A plus at the start of each heading is a minus while its section is open, the title is in medium weight, and the panel is muted text lined up with the title. One item is open at a time, or several with `multiple`; `value`, `defaultValue` and `onValueChange` control it, `headingLevel` sets the headings' level, `hiddenUntilFound` lets find in page open a closed panel, and `skeleton` shows it as a skeleton. Disclosure stays for one optional detail; its page now points a list of questions to Accordion.
- AvatarGroup keeps the room its spread takes, so spreading under the pointer never changes its size: it no longer wraps a narrow row and flickers between bunched and spread. At rest its avatars gather toward the start of that room, or toward the end with the new `gather="end"`, for a group at the end of a row. The count of the rest shows at rest too, under the last avatar, upright and set as initials are, semibold at the avatar's initials size. Avatars in a group and the count no longer take a ring in the color of the surface below, and Avatar's `--env-avatar-ring` hook is gone; `--env-avatar-group-ring` now sets only the gap in a chosen avatar's ring. A new check rejects a hover or focus-within rule that changes layout in any component.
- ColorPickerButton takes a color property, `--env-color-picker-button-container`, which gives one button a fill through `style` or CSS; without it, the button keeps no fill of its own. In RichTextEditor, a selected table cell takes the selection color, `--env-color-bg-selected`, instead of the neutral muted fill, so it reads as selected rather than as a code block or reference.
- Dev mode, a new entry point for development only: `@envsemble/envui/dev`.
  - `installDevMode()` adds `window.envui.layout` to the page, a Dev mode button in the corner and the shortcut Alt+Shift+D. Load it with a dynamic `import()` behind a development check, such as `import.meta.env.DEV`; no other entry point imports it, so production builds contain none of it.
  - `envui.layout.describe()` writes the page's layout as text, for agents: every layout node (an element with `data-env-layout`) and open overlay with its size in rem and window class, whether it is a container and the thresholds its queries test, its display, gap, padding and grid columns, what scrolls and what sticks, and what is hidden, with identical siblings folded.
  - `envui.layout.check()` returns the problems it measured: content wider than a node that doesn't scroll, a child sticking out, an overlay off the window, layout that paints, a sticky node with nothing scrolling and squeezed text. `envui.layout.at(['22rem', '40rem'])` returns both at each width, or at each `{ width, height }`, measured synchronously by resizing the root and putting it back.
  - The button opens a 3D view of the page, a plane per node lifted by nesting, with its surfaces, padding and gaps, colours by kind or window class and a ruler of the thresholds queried, with a toolbar of view directions and a filter to the selected node, a responsive frame with device sizes that lays the page out at another width and height, and a light, dark or high-contrast theme of its own, which the page's theme doesn't follow; the nodes as a tree in a sidebar; and an inspector with the selected node's part, tokens, container and problems, and the readout. See [Dev mode](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/dev-mode.md).

- A resizable Drawer keeps each label and group heading on one line, so its items keep their height as people narrow it: one that doesn't fit ends in an ellipsis. A label cut short shows in full in a tooltip beside the drawer, on hover and keyboard focus; a heading cut short, which takes no focus, gives its full text as its `title`, shown on hover. A Drawer that isn't resizable still wraps a long label.
- RichTextEditor and MarkdownEditor draw their formatting toolbar as a Toolbar of ghost buttons in groups, with a line between each: text styles, lists, blocks, and links and tables, then Undo and Redo, then Attach files. Commands of one kind next to each other in `toolbar` share a group, and the default orders put inline code with the text styles. RichTextEditor's formatting buttons are toggles from the start, pressed while the formatting is on at the caret. The toolbar stays one tab stop, the arrow keys move across its groups, and it still scrolls sideways where it is wider than the editor.
- Field labels have part classes: `env-text-field-label` on TextField's and Textarea's label, `env-select-label` on Select's, `env-combobox-label` on Combobox's and `env-date-picker-label` on DatePicker's, MonthPicker's and TimePicker's, so a style or a test can address a label without an element selector.
- Text fields have no fill of their own: TextField, SearchField, Textarea, Combobox, the date and time pickers, MarkdownEditor, RichTextEditor and Field.Control take the background of what they sit on, such as a card, a sheet or a dialog, in every theme, and keep their hover, focus and invalid borders. The border is a new part, `env-text-field-border`, a fieldset over the well's edge whose legend opens a notch behind the floated label, right to left too, so the label needs no fill to hide the line. A focused field's floated label takes the accent's text color, `--env-color-fg-accent`, which keeps 4.5:1 on every surface. `--env-text-field-container` still gives one field a fill. In RichTextEditor, code blocks, a selected table cell and references take the neutral muted fill, `--env-color-bg-neutral-muted`, as inline code does, and ColorPickerButton has no fill either.
- The code theme changed: inline code, code blocks, code diffs and the code in Markdown use Shiki's `github-light-default` theme in the light theme and `github-dark-dimmed`, a grey dark theme, in the dark one, instead of the Pierre themes. Under high contrast (a Theme's `contrast="high"`, or `system` while `prefers-contrast: more`) they take `github-light-high-contrast` and `github-dark-high-contrast` rather than the standard themes with darker or lighter tokens, and load them only once code shows at high contrast. Inline code and code blocks sit on translucent layers of the text color, inline code on the neutral muted fill (`--env-color-bg-neutral-muted`) and blocks on a layer of their own, so they stand a step apart from the page, panes, cards, muted fills and list items, and popups alike, darker in the light theme and lighter in the dark one; diffs sit on an opaque muted grey, light in the light theme and blue-grey in the dark one; code in the dark theme is no longer near black. Diffs color added and removed lines with the themes' greens and reds. Every token still reaches 4.5:1, and 7:1 under high contrast, on the backgrounds it sits on, over each of those surfaces; a few token colors are a little darker in the light theme and lighter in the dark one for it. The themes come with Shiki, so the content and diff entries no longer need the optional peer `@pierre/theme`: remove it unless the application uses it itself. Corners, padding and the copy button are unchanged.
- Text that shows more on hover or focus takes a quiet dotted underline. A HoverCard's or Tooltip's trigger that is text, an inline text element such as a span, abbr or time, or Text, holding text, gets `env-hover-detail`: a dotted underline in the muted text color, 0.25em below the text, with the page's own cursor, and in forced colors the text color. Buttons, icon buttons, links, avatars and other triggers keep their own look. An application's own text component can take the class to match. RelativeTime's time, which shows the full date in a tooltip, takes it too.

  A HoverCard whose trigger is text without `tabIndex` warns in the console while developing, since keyboard focus can't reach it. Text that a server component renders, which reaches a HoverCard or Tooltip as its `p` with the `env-text` class, takes the underline too.

- The navigation and the top bar sit on a background instead of behind lines. Drawer, Rail, Bar and TopBar each fill a plain, square rectangle with the navigation's background, the default surface color (`--env-color-bg-default`), flush with the window's edges and the body in a Scaffold, with no divider line; on a surface, such as a Sheet, they take that surface's color. A new `background` prop, `'default' | 'page'` (type `NavigationBackground`, named as Surface's and Card's fills), puts one on the page's background with `background="page"`, so an application can give the navigation a background and leave the top bar on the page, or the other way round; `'default'` is the default. On a surface both take the surface's color, and a sticky TopBar stays opaque either way. TopBar no longer draws a hairline below it, and Bar none above it. Under forced colors, where the system paints no backgrounds, each keeps an edge toward the body.
- Bar, Rail and Drawer, the navigation forms: the navigation bar for compact windows, the rail for medium ones and the drawer from expanded, which Scaffold picks by window class.
  - Each takes the same array of destinations, `Destination` items: `{ label, icon, href or onSelect, count, group, current }`. A destination with `href` is a link through the `LinkProvider`'s link component, and one without is a button that runs `onSelect`.
  - Each renders a `nav` landmark, named "Main" from `messages.navigation.label` unless it has `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`, with `aria-current="page"` on the current destination, whose Material Symbol fills. A count shows on the destination, 999+ above 999, and its name says it, such as "Inbox, 3 new", from `messages.navigation.itemCount`.
  - Drawer heads each run of a `group` and shows the count at the item's end; its icons are optional (`DrawerDestination`). Rail and Bar show the icon in a pill-shaped indicator above the label, with the count on it, and keep the groups' order without headings. Bar shows five destinations at most: past five, the first four and More, a menu with the rest. Rail and Drawer take a `header` and a `footer`.
  - Their parts are `env-bar-*`, `env-rail-*` and `env-drawer-*`, the current item with `data-current`. Bar's indicator and label are `env-bar-icon` and `env-bar-text`, since 0.x's Meter used `env-bar-indicator` and `env-bar-label`, which the codemods rename to `env-meter-*`; `v1-classes` renames 0.x's `env-bar` alone, and leaves the Bar's other parts.
  - Menu.Item takes `aria-current` and `aria-label`, which the Bar's More menu sets on its destinations. Drawer's group headings are headings, `h2` unless `headingLevel` gives another level.

- Panes.Trigger, a Button in the main pane that opens the supporting pane while it is a sheet, below `splitAt`. It says whether the sheet is open with `aria-expanded`, names the pane in `aria-controls`, and hides while the panes sit side by side, where the supporting pane shows anyway, so a Details button no longer lingers beside the details. Its part is `env-panes-trigger`.
- Panes, two panes side by side after Material's pane patterns: `behavior="supporting"`, Panes.Main and Panes.Supporting, whose supporting pane is a sheet over the main one in less room while `open`, a side sheet in a medium width and a bottom sheet in a compact one, which is modal, with a close button and everything else on the page inert, the window's own inside a Scaffold whose document scrolls, and which Escape, the button and the scrim close; and `behavior="list-detail"`, Panes.List and Panes.Detail, which show one at a time in less room, with a back button; the detail's content fills the room below the button, so a surface as tall as the pane (`min-block-size: 100%`) ends at its end rather than overflowing by the button's height. `splitAt` sets the window class of its own width from which they sit side by side, and CSS decides it. An optional Panes.Divider resizes the supporting pane or the list, in rem, by pointer and keyboard; `persist` keeps the width in a cookie, which `readPanesSize` reads on the server for `persistedSize`. `data-env-layout="panes"`, and the `env-panes` container.
- `PortalContainer` renders the popups, menus, tooltips, dialogs, sheets and toasts inside it into another element than the document's body, such as one in a shadow root or an iframe's document. With `contained`, they are part of that element, as in a preview of an interface with its overlays open: a modal one neither locks the page's scroll, traps focus nor hides the rest of the page, none takes focus as it opens, a popup opens on its own side at its content's size, toasts are shown without the page's F6 shortcut, and a press or Esc elsewhere doesn't close them, while their trigger and their own controls do. Select and Tooltip take `defaultOpen`.

  The component pages show overlays open in their figures: Dialog, ConfirmDialog, CommandPalette, Menu, Tooltip and Toast have anatomy figures, and their do and don't pairs show the open overlay; Select and Combobox show their open lists. A figure's fence takes the flag `open` for this, as `docs/component-pages.md` describes.

- Lists in formatted text, which Markdown, RichText and RichTextEditor render, take envUI's own markers instead of the browser's discs: a small circle in the selected fill on the middle of each item's first line, holding a dot for a bullet and the number for an ordered item, with a full step between items. A nested list steps down quietly, with closer items and a smaller marker without the fill, a muted dot or a letter. Task lists keep their checkboxes.
- Drawer and side Sheets can be resized by their inner edge with `resizable`: a handle there, the same as Panes.Divider's, that the pointer drags and the arrow keys move, 1 rem a step and 5 with Shift, with Home and End for the least and the largest width and a double click back to the default. It looks and behaves as Panes.Divider does: a grip of three dots at its middle, never a line, that darkens on hover and takes the focus color with keyboard focus and while dragging, when focus also rings it, with a wider touch target under a coarse pointer. A side sheet's grip stays at the middle of the sheet as its content scrolls. The width is in rem, between `minWidth` and `maxWidth`: `defaultWidth` sets where it starts, and `width` with `onWidthChange` lets the application control it and keep it, such as for the next visit. A Drawer takes at most half its container, such as the Scaffold; a side Sheet keeps to the window, and a bottom sheet ignores `resizable`. Sheet's `width` also takes a number of rem; a resizable side sheet ignores a CSS length there, and warns of it while developing. The handles are `env-drawer-resize-handle` and `env-sheet-resize-handle`, and `messages.navigation.resize`, `messages.navigation.width`, `messages.dialog.resize` and `messages.dialog.width` name them and their value. The handle reports the width in rem with its fraction, and as its largest value what the room allows. A `width` that is not a finite number, such as `NaN` from a stored value that didn't parse, is ignored. Without `resizable`, nothing changes.
- Scaffold, the invisible structure of an application's screen, after Material's: a top bar region, the navigation and the body. The document scrolls by default, as on any website, with the top bar and the bar sticking to the window's edges and the rail or drawer kept in view; `scroll="body"` scrolls the body alone under the top bar, for previews, embedded frames, title-bar windows and Panes whose panes scroll on their own. It shows one navigation form at a time by its own width, in CSS: the Bar at the bottom of a compact width, the Rail at the start of a medium one and the Drawer from expanded, from `navigation={{ bar, rail, drawer }}`, and `forms` changes which form each window class shows. It renders skip links to the body and the navigation, the banner and main landmarks, keeps clear of the safe areas, moves focus to the form that shows when the form that had it hides, and, where the body scrolls, lets the keyboard scroll it as it would the document. `--env-scaffold-sticky-top` keeps sticky content in the body under the top bar, and where the document scrolls, its scroll padding is the bars' heights, which the Scaffold measures, so focus stays clear of them. `data-env-layout="scaffold"`, and the `env-scaffold` container. `resolveNavigationForms` returns the form of each window class.
- SegmentedControl and Tabs look clearly different, after Material, with their APIs unchanged. SegmentedControl, which picks a value, is connected segments in one outline, the chosen one in the selected fill, `--env-color-bg-selected`, with a check before its label in place of its icon (a new part, `env-segmented-control-item-check`); a new color property, `--env-segmented-control-outline`, sets the outline, and the fill behind the options is transparent by default. Tabs, which switch panels, are text tabs on no fill, whatever their buttons' variants: the selected one in the accent's text color with an indicator under it, at the start in a vertical rail. New color properties `--env-tabs-selected-content` and `--env-tabs-indicator` set the selected label and the indicator, and the rail is transparent unless `--env-tabs-container` sets it.
- Sheet: only a finger swipes a sheet closed. A mouse or a pen no longer drags the sheet with the pointer or closes it with a swipe, as no desktop panel moves that way; a drag inside the sheet selects its text instead. Touch keeps the swipe toward the sheet's edge, and a drag on a resizable side sheet's handle resizes it, by touch too.
- Skeletons shimmer instead of pulsing: a soft highlight sweeps across every bar and shape, laid out on the window so it crosses every skeleton on the page as one, in step, and keeps still with a reduced-motion preference. The highlight is a new token, `--env-color-skeleton-shine`. Bars for missing text have small rounded corners on every line, shapes keep their component's corners, and media without corners of its own takes small ones. A Card over a placeholder item keeps the size of its loaded text: its title and meta share one row and its description is one line.
- Slider is a field: as tall as the fields and with their corners, so it sits in a form among them. Its label sits inside at the start, its value at the end, both in a field's type, the value with numbers of one width, and a fill from the start shows the value; a thin handle at the value and faint ticks at the steps, or at the large steps where the steps are many, appear under the pointer and while it has focus, and keyboard focus rings the whole field. Pressing or dragging anywhere in it chooses a value; the keyboard and accessibility stay Base UI's. The text keeps its full strength: where the handle and the end of the fill pass under the label or the value, they fade instead. The track has no outline. `--env-slider-indicator` now colors the fill alone, and a new color property, `--env-slider-content`, the label, the value, the handle and the ticks. The root marks `data-ticks` while it draws ticks.
- Avatars without a picture take a new pastel palette of tonal pairs: each of the eight hues is a soft fill, `--env-color-avatar-bg-{hue}`, with initials and a figure in a deep shade of the same hue, new tokens `--env-color-avatar-fg-{hue}` and `--env-color-avatar-figure-{hue}`, rather than white. The colors are powder blue, seafoam, sage, butter, blush, periwinkle, orchid and a warm oat. Initials keep 4.5:1 on their fill and 7:1 at high contrast; the dark theme takes a slightly deeper fill. A new prop, `selected`, shows an avatar as chosen with a ring and a check at its bottom end in the accent of a checked box (a new part, `env-avatar-check`), a look only that the control around it announces; an AvatarGroup person takes `selected` too, and comes in front of its neighbours so its check shows. Where a pointer can hover, AvatarGroup rests bunched, each avatar a little off the line and turned by its place, with the count of the rest under the last, and spreads into a row with gaps along a gentle arch under the pointer or while focus is inside; pressable avatars bunch less, so each keeps a target of at least 1.5 rem uncovered, with the motion tokens and at once with a reduced-motion preference.
- TopBar, the bar at the top of an application or a screen, built from Row: start content, a title that truncates first and actions, at the top bar height (`--env-top-bar-height`). `sticky` keeps it at the top as the page scrolls, `loading` shows page progress along its bottom edge, and `titleBar` makes it the window's own title bar in an installed web app.
- `useModifierKey()` gives the platform's shortcut modifier: `{ key: 'Meta', label: '⌘' }` on Apple platforms and `{ key: 'Control', label: 'Ctrl' }` elsewhere, for a Kbd's keys and `aria-keyshortcuts`. It gives Ctrl on the server and while the page hydrates, then ⌘ on Apple platforms, so a server-rendered search trigger hydrates without a mismatch. The search trigger pattern, its examples and the migration note use it in place of reading `navigator` while rendering.

### Patch Changes

- The soft accent badge takes a tint of its own, `--env-color-bg-accent-badge`: in the light theme the accent blue at 5%, fainter than `--env-color-bg-accent-muted` at 12%, so its blue text keeps 4.5:1 on list rows and muted surfaces. The blue itself is unchanged. In dark and at high contrast the new token has the value of `--env-color-bg-accent-muted`, and info alerts keep `--env-color-bg-accent-muted` in every theme. The ESLint plugin and the Stylelint config accept the new token.
- AvatarGroup rests as a compact pile, like stacked cards, each avatar a little off the line and turned, and the pile is one pointer target: hovering it, a tap on it or keyboard focus inside it spreads the group along its arch, and its avatars take presses and hovers only once the spread has ended, so no avatar under another can be pressed (WCAG 2.5.8). This replaces the looser rest that kept 1.5 rem of each pressable avatar uncovered. On touch screens, where groups used to stay a plain row, the first tap spreads a group, marked with `data-spread`, and later taps reach its avatars; a tap outside it or five seconds without a tap pile it up again. Keyboard focus spreads a group only with a focus ring, so a click no longer holds it spread after the pointer leaves. The count of the rest is a rounded square the size of the avatars, with corners from the corner scale, at the bottom of the pile.
- Breadcrumbs' focus ring stands 0.125 rem clear of its level's label, and the chevrons keep room for it on either side. Panes' back button keeps clear of the detail pane's corner: its label lines up 1 rem in, where a pane's content usually starts.
- Button's `end` slot takes the button's own content color instead of the muted text color, which fell to about 1.6:1 on a primary button in the light theme and 1.1:1 in the dark one, and ignored `--env-button-content`. Text in the slot is the label's color; keys in it are outlined caps with no fill, as in a tooltip, and the + between them is 80% of the label's color, which the contrast gate holds to 3:1 on every variant, tone and state in all four themes. Kbd takes `--env-kbd-separator-fg` for the color of the + between keys.
- `--env-radius-full` is capped: half the tallest single-line control, the large control height or the touch height, whichever is more, instead of an endless pill corner. Everything with rounded ends takes it, so any element that grows taller than one line, such as a button whose label wraps, a Combobox whose chips wrap, a badge, a switch or a navigation item, keeps the same corners and becomes a rounded rectangle; parts that are always square, such as avatars and round icon buttons, stay circles.
- The chevron of a Select or Combobox trigger flips over as its popup opens and closes, instead of turning half a circle. The half turn swung it through pointing sideways within a frame or two, so the trigger seemed to twitch. With a reduced-motion preference it still points the other way at once.
- CodeDiff shares CodeBlock's code theme: its tokens take exactly a code block's colors in the light, dark and high-contrast themes, and it sits on a code block's translucent layer instead of an opaque grey, so it stands a step apart from a page, card or popup as a block does. Added and removed lines are tinted with their change color at an opacity over that layer, chosen so that every token keeps 4.5:1, or 7:1 under high contrast, on the line and on a changed word. As the theme switches, a diff keeps its expanded lines and scroll, showing its previous colors until the new ones load.
- Combobox in a contained PortalContainer leaves the rest of the page in reach of assistive technology while its list is open, as the other contained overlays do. Elsewhere its open list still hides the page around it, as before.
- The pages of RichText, Markdown, Code, Badge, DescriptionList, Kbd, AnimatedNumber, Rating, RelativeTime, Disclosure, Avatar, Message, CalendarHeatmap, SearchTrigger, Toolbar and Link are on the figure template: visual first, with do and don't pairs, an anatomy figure from the part names and, where the component adapts, an example at several widths.
- The Button and Card documentation pages are on a new figure template: plain sections in a fixed order (Overview, Variants, When to use, Anatomy, Adapting to width, Accessibility), visual first, with short text that says each thing once. Do and don't figures are fences such as ` ```tsx do=OnePrimaryAction caption="…" `, and an anatomy figure's legend names each part by its class name. Every figure carries its example's whole source in the packaged pages, as examples do. The other component pages follow as they are converted.
- An inline confirmation's armed label is short and has no question mark, such as "Remove file": ConfirmButton's documentation, the destructive actions pattern and the content guidelines say so, and their examples follow. ConfirmButton announces its armed state as "Remove file. Press again to confirm.", with a full stop after the confirm label.
- DatePicker and TimePicker keep their minimum width, 12 rem, in a container sized to its content, such as an inline-flex toolbar holding a field and a button. Before, the container measured the field narrower than its minimum, the field then grew to it and pushed its neighbours onto another row, so the field needed a width of its own there. In a container narrower than 12 rem the field still takes the container's width.
- Dev mode's responsive frame is off until the reader turns it on with Responsive frame in the 3D view's toolbar, or picks a device: until then the page is laid out at its own size, with no frame, handles or label. Turning it off, or the frame's ×, brings the page back to its own size.
- Dev mode takes the theme of the page it measures as it opens: the theme boundary around its `root`, rather than the document's first. A page in a nested `Theme` of another theme than the document's, such as an example shown in a light theme on a dark page, has dev mode's toolbar, side columns, popovers and view in its theme too. Dev mode theme in the toolbar still sets another.
- Focus rings keep clear of their labels. A text Button's hit area and ring reach 0.25 rem past its label on each side, so the round ring no longer cuts into the first and last letters, and a negative inline margin takes that back, so its label lines up with the text around it as before (not where it fills its container with `fullWidth`). A ghost Button's ring traces the edge its state layer fills (`outline-offset: 0`), rather than floating 2 px past a shape nobody sees, so it is no larger than a filled button's.
- Fewer, fuller pages: Bar and Rail are documented on Drawer's page, with one list of destinations in three forms, and PageHeader on Page's. The feature cards pattern is the feature card on Card's page, and the search trigger pattern is the button and shortcut that open the palette, on CommandPalette's page. The old pages' addresses, on the Site and in the Docs, open the pages that hold them now.
- Text with more on hover, a Tooltip's or HoverCard's text trigger and RelativeTime, takes a clearer dotted underline: larger round dots, spaced further apart, drawn by one rule, `env-hover-detail`, and the pointer stays the arrow over it rather than the text cursor. RelativeTime's time with a tooltip now takes the underline, as its documentation said; before, its own part class kept it off.
- The dotted underline of a HoverCard's or Tooltip's trigger is only for text: a trigger with a role other than text's, such as an image or a button, or another component's part, such as an avatar group's "+2" count, takes none.
- The pages of Box, Row, Column, Grid, Surface, List, Scaffold, Panes, Page, PageHeader, TopBar, Bar, Rail, Drawer, Tabs, Pager, Tree, DocumentTabs, PanelDock, Breadcrumbs, Pagination, Sheet, Popover, HoverCard and Table are on the figure template: visual first, with do and don't pairs, an anatomy figure from the part names where the component paints, and examples at several widths where it adapts, such as Scaffold showing the bar, the rail and the drawer, Panes both behaviors in less room and Page its gutters.
- In the light theme at standard contrast, focus rings and muted text are a step darker, so they keep their contrast on light gray surfaces as well as on white. On a surface a little grayer than the page, about 0.035 lower in OKLCH lightness, a focus ring inside a pressed tab reached only 2.81:1 and a pressed tab's muted label 4.46:1. `--env-color-border-focus` goes from `#1872e7` to `#0366da`, 3.30:1 there, and `--env-color-fg-muted` from `#54575c` to `#4b4d51`, 5.21:1 there. `--env-color-fg-muted-hover` and `--env-color-fg-muted-pressed` move one step darker with it, so hovered and pressed muted text still gains contrast. The dark theme and both high-contrast themes are unchanged.
- The light theme's backgrounds mirror the dark theme's, so the same elements stand apart in both: what is lighter than its surroundings in dark is grayer in light. The page stays white; the default and raised surfaces (`--env-color-bg-default`, `--env-color-bg-raised`) are a cool gray step from it, and cards, other muted surfaces (`--env-color-bg-muted`) and list rows (`--env-color-list-item-bg`, now the muted surface in both themes) a further step, so a card stands apart from the page about four times as clearly as before. Hover and pressed states, the translucent neutral fill of secondary buttons, tab rails and chips (`--env-color-bg-neutral-muted`, now 10%) and dividers (`--env-color-border-muted`) follow. So that text keeps 4.5:1 on the grayer surfaces, link text (`--env-color-fg-link`, `--env-color-fg-link-on-tint`), muted text (`--env-color-fg-muted`) and the pressed edge of an invalid field are one grade darker; the accent, primary, selection and focus blues are unchanged. Popups (`--env-color-bg-popup`) stay white, so they stand apart from the panes they open over, as they do in dark; dialogs, sheets and the command palette now take the popup background too, so every overlay shares one surface. The dark theme and both high-contrast themes are unchanged, and no token is renamed.
- In the light theme the success, warning, danger and done text colors are one grade darker (`--env-color-fg-success` #02652b, `--env-color-fg-warning` #7e4b00, `--env-color-fg-danger` #ac190f, `--env-color-fg-done` #7032bf), so soft badges and diff stats keep 4.5:1 over their tint on list rows and muted surfaces. The accent blue is unchanged.
- A Link in running text keeps the spaces and punctuation beside it: the negative margin that lines a text button's label up with the text around it no longer applies to Link, which has no padding to take back.
- Markdown names a table without a caption or a heading before it after its first column's header, such as Variant table, with `messages.markdown.tableNamed`, rather than Table, so separate Markdown blocks on one page give their tables names of their own. `messages.markdown.table` names a table without a header row.
- Components measure in script only where CSS can't decide, and render usably before they measure.
  - CalendarHeatmap starts a narrow scroller at the most recent weeks through CSS (a reversed row, whose scroll origin is its end) instead of a ResizeObserver, so the server's markup and the first paint already show them; new data still scrolls back to them.
  - CodeDiff measures its width as the diff loads, before it paints, so a wide diff no longer shows the unified layout for a frame before it splits. Split and unified are different markup, so the width stays measured.
  - Tree renders 24 rows before it has measured its viewport, such as in the server's markup, so a tall tree's first paint is already full; measuring only adds rows below.
  - Composer follows its width with a ResizeObserver only where the browser can't size the field to its text (`field-sizing: content`).

- Hairlines separate the items of one list, always across the full width, and no longer divide regions. Menu.Separator is a hairline across the menu's whole width, edge to edge rather than inset from its sides, with space above and below, in menus, context menus, menubar menus and submenus. Markdown rules (`hr`) and table rows keep their lines. Lines that only divided regions are gone: DocumentTabs' bar has no base line, and the command palette's footer no line above it. A sticky PageHeader and a docked panel's header take the muted fill, `--env-color-bg-muted`, once content scrolls beneath them, instead of a line, and keep a line in forced colors. Panes.Divider is the gap between the panes, with a small grip of three dots in it, darker on hover and in the focus color with keyboard focus, which also rings the gap, and while dragging; it draws no line. Give each pane a Surface so they stand apart across the gap. Toolbar.Separator keeps its line between groups. Dev mode's inspector sits beside the view without a line. No class, prop or token changes: where an application needs a line, give the part its own `border`.

  Migration guide: [Navigation and collections](https://github.com/envsemble/envUI/blob/main/docs/migration.md).

- Numbers and editor toolbars keep their width in narrow places.
  - A Table cell with `numeric` no longer breaks a number at its separators, such as `$4,687.50` after the comma: where the columns keep their widths, the column stays as wide as its numbers and the region scrolls.
  - The formatting toolbar of MarkdownEditor and RichTextEditor sizes its row of buttons to the buttons, so where they don't fit, the toolbar scrolls the whole row rather than the buttons spilling out of it.

- The outermost Theme, the `env-window` container, is a block with `box-sizing: border-box`: one that renders an inline element now takes its parent's width, so its container queries match, and one with padding stays inside its parent instead of overflowing it. Any class an application sets overrides both, as before.
- PanelDock is marked experimental: its API and how it decides stacking may still change.
- PanelDock's minimized tabs float over the bottom edge of the dock instead of taking a strip of their own, so minimizing or bringing back a panel no longer moves the open ones. Open panels reach the dock's bottom, and their room for `height` and `maxHeight` is the dock's height below the gutter. Their content keeps the tabs' height clear at its end, with scroll padding to match, so its last lines and actions scroll out from beneath the tabs; content that fills the panel ends above them.
- PanelDock stacks from a container query, so the server's markup of a narrow dock already stacks and the first paint matches what the dock shows once it hydrates, without a jump. The dock is now an inline-size container: an unnamed `@container` query in panel content resolves against it, and its own font size is `stackBelow` / 37.5, which panels and tabs reset.
- The pattern pages, app frame, account menu, window frame, navigation, forms, settings, destructive actions, feedback, empty states, filtering, data tables and progressive disclosure, are on the figure template: shorter, with do and don't pairs and an anatomy figure of the composition where it has parts to name. The foundation pages say each point once, and the dev mode page is shorter, with real output of `describe()` and `check()` and the skeleton shift check.
- The pattern pages keep their decision and their compositions, and link to each component for its own behaviour. The window frame is App frame's "An installed app's window", and the account menu and the organization switcher are one pattern, Account and organizations, at `docs/patterns/account-and-organizations.md`; their old addresses open the pages they joined. Each pattern's decision is a Which to use table. Form layout and validation adds a long form in steps, with Progress split into steps, and which control to choose for a choice or a date; Empty states adds a whole page, such as one not found. The content guidelines are the Writing guide, `docs/writing.md`: their labels and error messages live on Form layout and validation, their empty states on Empty states, their feedback on Notifications and feedback and their confirmations on Destructive actions.
- DatePicker, MonthPicker and TimePicker:
  - The actions under a popup, Today, This month, Now and Clear, keep their labels on one line; where the popup is too narrow for them side by side, the row wraps whole buttons.
  - A mouse wheel turns a time wheel one row a notch, and a touchpad as far as it scrolls, easing into place, rather than as far as a page would scroll; quick notches add up to one turn.
  - Switching between AM and PM keeps the hour and minutes and moves the time by 12 hours, also while another wheel is still settling, and each choice reaches `onValueChange` once.

- Progress and Meter draw their track's edge at 3:1 against the surface, as a switch that is off does, so the track's extent stays clear in the light theme. A segmented Progress draws each segment's fill as `.env-progress-segment::before`, over that edge.
- Prose lists, in Markdown, RichText and RichTextEditor, drop the tinted circle: a bullet is a plain small dot in the text color, on the middle of the item's first line, and an ordered item's number is plain, in tabular figures in the muted text color. One level in, a small muted ring or a letter.
- Meter's and Progress's tracks, and Progress's segments, lose their outlines: the track is a quiet tint, as Material draws it, and the indicator keeps 3:1 against it and the surface on every surface, a card's included. The value text says the exact amount.
- Component and pattern pages name each related component or page once: the frontmatter's `related` list goes, and `avoidWhen` is the one list, each entry naming an export in `use` or a page in `see` with when to pick it instead. The packaged pages' Related section lists those entries, and the manifest's `related` is the pages they name. Do and don't pairs that state the obvious leave the pages and their examples.
- A tall element never keeps pill corners: Button, the single-line fields (TextField, SearchField, Combobox, the pickers and Field.Control), the command palette's search, Drawer's items, a disabled control's tooltip target and a Surface with `radius="full"` take a radius of half their single-line height rather than a full one, so a Combobox whose chips wrap, a button whose label wraps or a toolbar whose controls wrap keep the same corners and become rounded rectangles.
- A Select that opens with focus still on its trigger, when Base UI's move of focus to the highlighted option is lost on a busy page, moves focus to that option once the list has opened, so the arrow keys and Enter act on the list.
- Select moves focus to the highlighted option, where Base UI's queued focus was lost, only when the list opened from the keyboard; a list opened with the pointer keeps focus where Base UI leaves it.
- envUI's documentation is hosted in two parts. The Site, at the root, introduces envUI: its components, patterns and foundations, with a Benchmark and a Changelog of short release notes. The Docs, under `/docs`, are the developer reference: setup, migration, the codemods, agent tooling, dev mode, the foundations' guides, the design tokens and each component's props, part names and changelog. The top bar links one to the other, and search finds the pages of both. The offline catalogue, `envUI.html`, and the archived versions built from it are gone, so a release no longer attaches it; a hash address of an earlier release opens its page at the plain path.
- Skeletons look the same throughout: text that is there, such as a heading that doesn't wait for data, draws as rounded bars over its line boxes that shimmer with the rest, rather than a square line through it, which stays only until the script measures the text, as from a server. The skeleton's own surfaces, such as a card or a list row, carry a fainter highlight in step with the bars. A root that scrolls, such as a wide skeleton Table or a tall skeleton Dialog, draws the bars past its box too. The root takes `data-env-skeleton-text` while it draws them.
- Slider at rest shows only its label, its value and its fill, which ends crisply. A thin handle at the value and faint ticks at the steps show while a pointer hovers or presses it, and go once it is released, even with the pointer still over it, until the pointer leaves and comes back; a finger shows them only while it is down. Keyboard focus shows the handle alone, with the focus ring. While they show, where the handle, the end of the fill and the ticks pass under the label or the value, they fade, and the text keeps its full strength. A release outside the slider, a cancelled pointer, a lost pointer capture or the window losing focus all end a press. The slider's control marks `data-handle` while the handle shows, `data-ruler` while the ticks do and `data-pressed` while the pointer presses it.
- CodeDiff's button for changes that could not be shown reads Try again instead of Retry, as envUI's writing guide words it; `messages.codeDiff.retry` still overrides it.

## 1.0.0-prerelease.0 — 2026-10-18

The first prerelease of envUI 1.0, published under the `prerelease` dist-tag; 1.0.0 follows after further changes.
